## Full-stage acceptance audit — 2026-10-16

Audit entry: `32e0127177e4182ec05213ebef02a582809c91c9` (clean checkout, **418/418** fixed cases).
Reviewed the changes after the previous independent audit at `22a31181`,
including `0c5fbb16`, `daec6696` and `32e01271`, against the frozen C++ owners,
then reviewed and validated correcting implementation `3767c2f31071b5c38ed4a7479183e3d496ff1993`.
The preceding goal turn was progress: its variable-template declaration group
closed the last fixed failure. This audit found additional defects despite the
passing fixed manifest; acceptance below rests on source responsibilities,
demand/ownership review and expanded probes as well as the gates.

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the full PA14 handout, the clone plan,
source map and this ledger; consulted the frozen implementation directly.
PA12's inherited class/lifetime boundary and PA22's member-pointer boundary were
also checked in their owning handouts. The scope is the complete PA14 first-tier
contract plus every represented frozen PA14 case, including extended declaration
checks. It does not promote the later template tiers into this assignment.

### Responsibility and completeness review

`responsibility-review.json` in the evidence root records fourteen reviewed
behavior groups, C++ owners, Rust owners and the control-flow/ownership reasoning.
The source map records the concrete ownership changes. The review includes:

| C++ responsibility | Rust ownership and reviewed boundary |
| --- | --- |
| `class_templates.cpp` head/default merging, argument formation, canonical shells and current instantiation | `templates.rs`, `template_names.rs`, `class_templates.rs`, `types.rs`: positional parameters, declaration-owned defaults/limits and canonical primary/argument keys; shell formation does not complete a class or demand bodies |
| `function_templates.cpp` substitution, deduction, specialization and candidate reuse | `function_templates.rs`, `dependent_members.rs`, `template_operands.rs`: lexical substitution, canonical adjusted parameters, cv/reference rules, matching base specializations, complete signature keys and separate active class-depth membership |
| `analyzer_bodies.cpp`, `template_fixed_expressions.cpp`, lookup and dependent names | `expressions.rs`, `fixed_expressions.rs`, `member_patterns.rs`, `dependent_members.rs`: definition-time failures, name categories, current/enclosing scopes, fixed versus dependent bases and ordinary indexed lookup/access rules |
| `class_templates.cpp` class/member/static/enum definitions and definition frames | `instance_definitions.rs`, `template_bodies.rs`, `member_patterns.rs`, `enum_instances.rs`, `variable_instances.rs`: named and nested identities, renamed outside heads, source links, selective definition/initializer/storage demand and in-progress/completed states |
| `ast_clone.cpp` and instantiation context | `syntax/cloning.rs`, `template_context.rs`: retained parsed patterns, fresh fact-bearing IDs, unit slabs, parent-linked frames, complete Result restoration and separate logical/native limits |
| `instantiate_default_argument`, `function_nothrow`, `noexcept_value` | `template_defaults.rs`, `default_arguments.rs`, `template_exceptions.rs`, `exceptions.rs`: defaults and exception specifications are independent, memoized demands with declaration-owned scopes and cv-correct prototype objects |
| Ordinary PA11–PA13 copy/move, conversion, destructor and virtual reuse | Existing class owners plus `allocation.rs`, `copy_construction.rs`, `destruction.rs`, `template_bodies.rs`: selected typed plans, lazy unused members, outside destructor availability and ordinary class-result/value-category rules |
| `analyzer_calls.cpp` and allocation overloads | `procedural_calls.rs`, `function_templates.rs`, `allocation.rs`: ordered candidates, implicit typed size prefix, selected body demand, written placement operands and the frozen omitted-default boundary |
| `semantics_dump.cpp` and declaration specifiers | `semantics_dump.rs`, `specifiers.rs`: exact generic delete view, outer class-copy conversion view and virtual/explicit/mutable failure precedence |
| `object_lowering.cpp`, `cleanup_lowering.cpp`, `condition_lowering.cpp`, symbols and definition order | `lower/materialization.rs`, `lifetime_regions.rs`, `lifetime.rs`, `expressions.rs`, `symbols.rs`, `definition_order.rs`: exact temporary/cleanup region order, callable allocated copies, ABI variants, weak/inline/unwind metadata and separate emission ordering |

The accepted earlier reviews supply the retained class/static/enum/dependent-name
translations; this audit rechecked their integration with the completed first
tier and the newer declaration groups. The final frozen manifest has no missing
or failing identity. The remaining template implementation boundaries named by
older partial entries are superseded for the PA14 responsibilities above;
valid packs, non-type arguments, explicit/partial specialization use, general
member/friend templates and alias/variable-template specialization demand remain
with their later owning assignments. They retain explicit missing-implementation
statuses when actually required, not invented successful rejections.

### Findings corrected

1. **Base-specialization deduction was absent.** Direct calls and pointer/reference
   arguments now use the canonical adjusted parameter type and the frozen
   `deduce_from_base` fallback. A query visits each base identity once, reuses its
   trial vector, stops a matching branch and rejects conflicting deduced argument
   vectors. Selected conversions still own accessibility/subobject ambiguity.
   Array/function parameter adjustment, forwarding, const qualification, indirect
   bases, conflicting arguments and the pointer-reference exclusion were checked.
2. **Instance member defaults were typed eagerly.** Class copies retain their own
   default operands, class ScopeId and definition limit. Only a selected omitted
   parameter receives facts; `(EntityId, parameter index)` has explicit active and
   completed states. Methods, static methods, constructor calls, default construction,
   nested classes and unevaluated calls use the existing owners. Default-constructor
   semantic dumps retain C++'s action-only boundary; lowering demands its arguments.
3. **Signature-demanded noexcept returned 86.** Declaration-only, addressed and
   body-defined specializations now resolve their specification independently in
   a declaration-owned prototype frame. The entity's Evaluating/Value state handles
   recursion and memoization, failures restore the pattern, and later body/emission
   demand reuses the fact. An unresolved exception fact at the immutable lowering
   boundary is an invariant failure. Unused invalid bodies remain uninstantiated.
4. **Prepared outside destructors looked available too early.** A separate sparse
   availability fact preserves the original pre-demand inertness query despite
   early semantic preparation. Inline bodies, earlier explicit uses, defaulted
   members, nested/base/member destruction and explicit roots retain their own
   boundaries. Calls, aliases and object-root metadata now match the frozen bytes.
5. **Implicit allocation calls could not deduce function templates.** The shared
   matcher now receives the typed size argument and written placement operands;
   it creates no synthetic token/expression. Selection requests the specialization's
   body. The placement path converts only written operands and preserves C++'s
   omission of unwritten defaults; those raw probes do not claim native validity
   for an omitted-default allocation call. The required basic placement control
   also passes the original native evaluator.
6. **Earlier dump/specifier paths lost parity.** Delete expressions now print the
   frozen generic view; a selected class copy projects its outer conversion while
   retaining its inner binding for lowering. Reference-to-class initializer name
   checks apply the frozen `IsClassObjectType` suppression. Plain `explicit` no
   longer hits a stub: non-function checks follow virtual and precede mutable/
   typedef restrictions. C++'s unusual acceptance of plain explicit ordinary
   functions is preserved, including declaration and diagnostic order.
7. **Inherited ordinary object lowering differed.** Inert temporary completion now
   closes/reopens the construction segment, preserves existing temporary chains,
   opens locally extended call results before their addresses and preserves a
   reopened conditional operand's segment. A private per-function `ChoiceRegion`
   restores across Result propagation. Allocated glvalue copies retain callable
   constructor plans before the graph becomes immutable; ordinary storage-copy
   rules remain distinct. The old reduced cleanup/copy boundaries in this ledger
   now match, and the previously passing PA12 conditional destination case is retained.

Three new unit tests verify default syntax/fact ownership and memo reuse,
exception-demand independence from bodies, and outside-definition availability
independently of prepared syntax. Ten new PA14 controls are in `tests/controls/`:
`template-array-parameter-adjustment.t`, `template-deduction-base-paths.t`,
`template-deduction-base-conflicting-arguments-bad.t`,
`template-deduction-pointer-reference-base-bad.t`,
`template-destructor-outside-empty.t`, `template-member-default-demand.t`,
`template-member-default-demand-bad.t`, `template-noexcept-independent-demand.t`,
`template-noexcept-independent-demand-bad.t` and
`template-placement-allocation-deduction.t`.
The two inherited regressions belong to PA12's executed controls lane:
`allocated-defaulted-trivial-copy.t` and `inert-class-result-cleanup-segments.t`.
No existing fixture, reference or benchmark was formatted or changed.

### Architecture and source quality

The production path still parses once and lowers typed facts directly. Class,
function, default, exception, storage and emission demands remain separate;
canonical keys include their owning declaration/enclosing specialization and
argument identities. New member-default context is immutable and unit-owned;
completed keys need no invalidation. Definition availability is separate from
inertness/emission memos. No global scan/retry, textual phase transport, rendered
semantic key, parser replay, new owning pointer graph or environment read was
added. Base-query worklists and trial storage end with the query; cleanup and
choice state end with the function. Numeric conversion, target wrap/truncation,
floating bits and array/sizeof arithmetic retain their shared typed owners.

The companion audit caught a 249-code-line declaration function during development.
The correction moved the cohesive virtual/explicit function-specifier query to
its specifier owner and removed the old virtual-only helper. It did not split an
implementation into mechanical chunks or add a lint allowance. The reopened-choice
state uses an enum, not an excessive extra boolean or a lint waiver. Final rustfmt,
strict all/pedantic Clippy, debug/release tests and authoritative source/function/
nesting/placement audit all pass. Optimizer, register allocator and native policy
were not modified. Required work through PA14 runs in Rust; the frozen native tool
is only the documented pre-PA24 LowIR evaluator. No production C++ fallback exists.

### Disposition and next boundary

**PA14 full-stage acceptance is proven for this reviewed implementation.** Its
handout responsibilities and frozen extended declaration obligations have Rust
owners, the extra due defects found by source/probe review are corrected, all
required lanes and entry case identities are retained, and the architecture/
quality/resource evidence is concrete. Later unsupported template features remain
classified; this is not a PA33 implementation-wide census or a PA34 object/tool
fixed-point proof. PA33 debug/release lane and census obligations remain due at PA33;
PA34 inception remains due at PA34. PA35 is not an active conversion stage.

The next assignment boundary is PA15's integral non-type parameters/arguments,
integral constant-expression metaprogramming and explicit specializations.
Ralph retains stage advancement and its existing goal; no Ralph state, acceptance
policy, oracle or goal identity was edited or replaced. The implementation and
this audited ledger are committed, with a clean handoff.

## Variable-template declaration patterns — 2026-10-16

Implementation entry HEAD: `daec6696`. The stage base and last audited commit
above remain unchanged. Entry inspection found a clean checkout and **417/418**
fixed cases passing. The preceding goal turn is progress: its committed
pack-formation group reduced the fixed failures **2 → 1**, retaining all entry
passes (see the preceding checkpoint's evidence). This entry implements a
coherent declaration group; it does not certify the entire template subsystem
or advance PA14. Ralph owns the independent checkpoint/final audit.

### Frozen owners and completed group

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the clone plan, handout and ledger;
consulted the frozen C++ owners directly. The sole entry failure,
`tests/general/200-variable-template-defaulted-partial.t`, diverged at
`variable_templates.cpp::declare_variable_template/variable_partial_specialization`:
Rust treated the partial's declarator as a redefinition of the primary variable.

- `variable_templates.rs` now registers and merges primary heads and defaults,
  and routes a variable template-id declarator to a separate partial pattern
  before ordinary object/specifier checks, matching
  `analyzer_declarations.cpp::declare_declarator/declare_variable`. Qualified
  lookup retains its normal owner/ambiguity behavior. A partial can have its own
  declared type and initializer; it never replaces or publishes an ordinary
  binding for the primary. A template-id outside a specialization declaration
  receives the frozen diagnostic.
- `template_names.rs` shares canonical type argument formation with class
  template consumers. `templates.rs` owns ordinary-type-head validation.
  `class_templates.rs::complete_type_template_arguments` shares the existing
  default substitution and its `(HeadId, index, preceding TypeId arguments)`
  memo. Forming a default does not complete a class or evaluate an initializer.
- `partial_specializations.rs` translates the ordinary-type part of
  `template_specializations.cpp::check_partial_arguments/deduced_parameters`:
  argument formation/default completion precedes no-default and own-parameter
  diagnostics, followed by unexpanded-pack and deducibility checks. The
  iterative visited `TypeId` worklist includes pointers, references, arrays,
  function results/parameters, member-pointer owners/elements and dependent
  class arguments. Nested-name specifiers and decltype placeholders are
  non-deduced. The existing `pack_formation.rs` query checks packs from enclosing
  heads as well, before reporting a parameter that cannot be deduced.
- `variable_instances.rs` retains each source initializer/declarator, definition
  `HeadId`, `ScopeId`, location and lookup limit independently of the canonical
  primary head. Later declarations without an initializer preserve the
  definition's parameter names and context. Partials retain canonical argument
  lists in per-primary source order and publish a dense declarator-to-pattern
  fact. Template-id declarations outside the active head scope retain a separate
  sparse deferred-declaration fact, without looking up a primary or publishing
  an object. The attribute pass consumes these facts only when no ordinary
  binding exists: both C++ paths leave `init.declared` null. Access settlement
  still runs.
- Definition-time initializer name checks use retained syntax without copying
  or evaluating it. Semantic bare variable-template names receive the frozen
  missing-arguments diagnostic. A specialization demand remains explicitly
  missing implementation (86), rather than treating the primary as an ordinary
  variable or reporting an invented language rejection.

Three new ownership/architecture unit tests verify primary/partial identity,
definition-scope retention across renamed redeclarations, untouched initializer
syntax/facts, absent storage demands and instance states, failed-pattern
publication/context restoration, and an iterative deducibility walk through a
2,048-level shared function-type DAG. Nine required regressions run in PA14's
controls lane:

- `variable-template-deferred-declarators.t`
- `variable-template-partial-patterns.t`
- `variable-template-partial-own-arguments-bad.t`
- `variable-template-partial-nondeduced-bad.t`
- `variable-template-partial-initializer-name-bad.t`
- `variable-template-partial-default-argument-bad.t`
- `variable-template-partial-outer-pack-bad.t`
- `variable-template-default-redeclaration-bad.t`
- `variable-template-name-without-arguments-bad.t`

The source map records each responsibility. The semantic unit owns all pattern
facts and typed indices; query worklists/visited sets die at the query boundary.
No source/token replay, rendered semantic keys, whole-program scan or retry,
cache invalidation, new dependency, environment read or backend state was added.
The production path retains one parsed syntax owner and the existing demand
boundaries. Lowering, optimizer, register allocator, emission order and
per-function backend lifetimes are unchanged.

## Declarator pack formation and unexpanded-name validation — 2026-10-16

Implementation entry HEAD: `0c5fbb16`. Stage base
`2cc3a56f91c91d58c0c1738bd7d2ef6e60842ceb` and last audited commit
`22a311810449cfeca84f3f557f4c46173b8040ae` remain unchanged. Entry inspection
found a clean checkout and **416/418** fixed cases passing. The preceding goal
turn is classified as no progress against the supplied stage-progress receipt
(**416/418 → 416/418**). Revalidated both raw semantic mismatches and took the
next available implementation action; the earlier committed default-declaration
checkpoint is retained. This is a coherent partial implementation checkpoint;
Ralph retains audit, goal execution and assignment advancement.

### Frozen owners, completed group and typed data flow

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the clone plan, PA14 handout and
current ledger; consulted the frozen C++ owners directly. The two entry failures
cluster at different semantic owners: `template_packs.cpp` /
`analyzer_types.cpp` for invalid declarator expansion formation, and
`variable_templates.cpp` for primary/partial declaration registration.

Completed the first owner's validation group, including its related declaration
invariants rather than stopping at the initial non-pack rejection:

- `declarators.rs` now applies the **root** declarator ellipsis after forming
  every pointer, array and function suffix, as
  `analyzer_types.cpp::declarator` does. Invalid type formation takes precedence
  over the pack diagnostic. A nested parenthesized marker is not promoted to
  the root: this preserves the frozen compiler's observable behavior.
- `pack_formation.rs` translates the emptiness query of
  `template_packs.cpp::unexpanded_packs/pack_expansion_type`. A visited typed
  worklist follows dependent-member qualifiers, dependent specialization
  arguments and dependent template-name owners, ordinary/member pointers,
  references, array elements/bounds and function result/parameter types. It
  distinguishes parameter-pack shape from ordinary dependency and never counts
  a template declaration's parameters as a specialization's arguments.
- `function_names.rs` translates
  `analyzer_declarations.cpp::declare_function`'s unexpanded-pack check for
  pack-owning function-template heads, before declaration publication. Its
  parameter/result walk deliberately excludes expression placeholders, as the
  frozen owner does. Name redeclaration errors precede pack validation.
- `pack_names.rs` translates `DefinitionPacks/expression_packs` and the
  `ast_clone.cpp::collect_packs` read-only view. It visits parsed expression/type
  IDs, looks up leading names in the definition scope, excludes member tails,
  and leaves nested expression/template-argument expansions and `sizeof...`
  names to their own owners. It includes casts, function type syntax,
  dependent bounds, attributes, initializers and exception/trailing-return
  type syntax. It neither clones syntax nor repeats grammar parsing.
- `template_operands.rs` captures pack membership **once at placeholder
  formation**, matching `template_placeholders.cpp::placeholder`. The immutable
  `Pattern.pack_names` fact is Known or explicitly Unsupported; expansion checks
  consume it without looking names up again after the definition point.
  Unsupported lambda/inline class or enum pack-name traversal stays an explicit
  missing-implementation result when required, never an empty pack set.

Three semantic unit tests check failure publication/context restoration,
pack identities through dependent owners and shared type DAGs (2,048 levels,
with repeated child edges), and definition-scope/nested-expansion isolation.
The last test changes the original lookup index after placeholder formation and
proves the stored pack fact survives while fresh lookup no longer finds the name.
Five required regressions are in PA14's executed `tests/controls/` lane:
`nonpack-qualified-ellipsis-bad.t`, `nonpack-decltype-ellipsis-bad.t`,
`unexpanded-template-pack-function-bad.t`,
`nonpack-ellipsis-type-error-first-bad.t`, and
`parenthesized-ellipsis-pattern.t`.

The source map records each translated responsibility. Pack queries own their
short-lived work/visited sets; each type DAG node is processed once per query.
Only pack-owning template heads request the function-type walk. Placeholder
name collection is local to the newly formed operand, as in C++; reused
placeholders reuse its fact. No whole-program sweep, cache invalidation,
rendered semantic key, new syntax ownership graph, dependency or environment
read was added. Failed checks publish no successful function or root declarator
fact and restore the active template context. LowIR lowering, emission order,
optimizer, register allocator and backend function lifetimes are unchanged.

## Independent audit of the accepted dependent-name, member-template and synthesis groups — 2026-10-16

Audit entry: `ab63debe24159e729060a644251797e5d996d819`. Reviewed the
entire implementation diff from `7705c87b` through the three accepted checkpoints
`29d55512`, `f2b42148` and `ab63debe`, then the corrections recorded here. The
preceding goal turn was progress: it committed the synthesized-member/class-result
group and reached 415/418. Entry inspection found a clean checkout and no live
compiler/gate process. Stage base is unchanged. The final last-audited marker
names the reviewed correction implementation, independently of this ledger's
bookkeeping commit. This certifies the represented partial groups, not PA14
completion or advancement.

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the clone plan, PA14 handout and
ledger, source map, and the PA17/PA18 handouts for regression placement. Consulted
the frozen C++ owners directly, including `template_names.cpp`'s dependent names,
stand-ins, substitution and outside frames; `class_templates.cpp`'s defaults,
member links, explicit roots and definition instantiation; `ast_clone.cpp`'s
Template declaration branch; `analyzer.cpp`'s template parameters;
`analyzer_declarations.cpp::default_definition`; `analyzer_copy.cpp`'s selected
copy facts; `analyzer_virtual.cpp::virtual_definition`; `analyzer_expressions.cpp`
and `constant_evaluator.cpp`'s class/decltype demand; and `object_lowering.cpp`,
`program_lowering.cpp` and `symbol_lowering.cpp`'s transfer, inertness, entry
emission and metadata rules.

### Findings corrected in the reviewed scope

1. **Shared dependent class-default syntax captured an earlier enclosing
   specialization's facts.** A copied member-class template shares its parameter
   clause; `class_default_type_in` still analyzed the original type syntax when
   earlier arguments remained dependent. A later enclosing specialization could
   therefore read the first scope's cached decltype/array-bound facts and lose a
   valid function candidate. The dependent branch now clones fact-bearing type
   syntax, forms its pattern under the default's recorded lookup limit, restores
   that limit on both Result paths, and substitutes in its declaration-owned
   frame. Successful `(HeadId, ordinal, canonical prefix)` memos remain unchanged.
   Inline, renamed outside and array-default probes now match in all three views.
   A unit test proves distinct default scopes share one immutable syntax ID while
   that original syntax retains no type facts.
2. **Synthesized emission roots were treated as semantic definition uses.**
   Explicit member roots and implicit lifetimes incorrectly diagnosed an unused
   out-of-class defaulted destructor during `--emit-semantics`. Only an actual
   semantic use now requests that synthesis in the semantic view; lowering still
   prepares it for emission. A unit-owned, EntityId-keyed waiting set resumes a
   visited request on its first later semantic-use event. It neither polls
   pending definitions nor loses a use discovered while another template body is
   instantiated. Unit tests distinguish an unused explicit root, direct semantic
   use and emission, and prove that a late destructor call resumes the waiting
   definition and diagnoses its deleted deallocator.
3. **Defaulted virtual destructor synthesis omitted deallocation selection.**
   Ordinary instantiated virtual-destructor reuse reached lowering without its
   required deallocator fact and returned internal error 70. Synthesis now invokes
   the existing typed `complete_virtual_deallocation` operation after the selected
   default definition, before publishing Synthesized. Successful, explicit-root,
   inline, custom-deallocator and deleted-deallocator paths match; failures remain
   hard instantiation errors with the frozen diagnostic bytes.
4. **Base/deleting entries inferred inline hints from weak demand linkage.**
   An out-of-class instance destructor acquired `inline_hint=yes` despite having
   no inline specifier. Entry metadata now uses the recorded inline preference,
   just as complete entries do; weak instantiation linkage remains independent.
5. **A defaulted move's named copy-initialization used the wrong transfer.**
   Reference-cast initializers of a synthesized out-of-class defaulted move
   emitted addresses plus `copyobj`, where C++ loads and stores the object value
   before taking the destination address. That named-storage branch now consumes
   the selected constructor's storage-transfer fact as well as Storage actions.
   A written direct move still calls its selected constructor.
6. **Allocated glvalue copies bypassed their required constructor call.**
   Applying the newly supported synthesized storage-copy path to `new A(a)`
   omitted the selected copy definition and construction-unwind deallocation.
   Allocation now consumes the retained Constructor action for a glvalue source
   and calls that exact plan; prvalue materialization remains separate. The new
   call, unwind region, deallocation and emission order match raw C++ LowIR.
7. **Dependent template-name substitution discarded a frozen selected binding.**
   Rust filtered `LookupResult::ambiguous`, whereas the C++ substitution owner
   consumes its first binding. That changed overload selection and accepted an
   expression C++ rejects. The translated owner now consumes the same recorded
   binding; ordinary lookup's ambiguity handling is unchanged. The regression
   pins the frozen behavior rather than changing the C++ oracle.
8. **Every storage-copy use rescanned the constructor's subobject actions.**
   The per-plan transfer property is now published as typed `CopyTransfer` with
   the selected plan and read in O(1) during lowering. Declaration-to-definition
   replacement preserves its ConstructorId; synthesis publishes the final
   property once without changing nontrivial ABI passing. The existing identity
   test verifies repeated demand, stable selected plan, storage-copy availability,
   no copied syntax and an undemanded second specialization.

Required regressions are in their earliest owning executed control lanes:

- PA14 `tests/controls/defaulted-member-transfer-audit.t` and
  `tests/controls/defaulted-virtual-deallocation-audit-bad.t` cover ordinary
  instantiated special-member reuse, allocation/copy transfer and virtual
  deallocation failure.
- PA17 `tests/controls/member-template-dependent-default-audit.t` covers copied

- PA18 `tests/controls/dependent-template-ambiguous-selection-audit.t` covers

All four pass their protected focused course invocation. All twelve raw
LowIR/type/semantic comparisons for these controls match, including rejection

written.

### Representation, rollback, demand and lifetime review

`Names` interns canonical `(TypeId, NameId)` identities and the ordinary class
specialization store interns canonical written arguments under the stand-in's
EntityId. Undefined stand-ins stay dependent and have no fabricated definition
or member scope. Concrete substitution resolves the template name before its
arguments, visits remaining written arguments after a soft failure, applies
concrete defaults only after lookup and preserves hard required-instantiation
errors. Failed trials publish no completed signature/default. Logical depth
1024 and native byte-budget exhaustion remain separate.

Copied declarations share immutable parameter clauses and retained body syntax;
parameter/default checking and demanded bodies receive fresh slab IDs for facts.
Source links pair copied declarations through their class syntax positions,
exclude namespace friend/self links, and only traverse those copied nested
classes. Link chains are acyclic append-only copy provenance. Enclosing frames
bind only retained parameter lists over the concrete owner; no whole visible
binding map is copied. In-progress and completed body states, complete canonical
keys and the new keyed semantic-use event prevent duplicate successful work.
Expected candidate rejection remains a compact Result; diagnostics are rendered
only when reported. Instantiation contexts restore lookup/access/body/dependent
use state on errors, and failed required body analysis terminates the owning
unit without exposing a successful definition.

Selected constructors retain direct IDs while synthesis fills their plans.
Lowering consumes those actions, recorded receivers, layouts, argument lists and
ABI facts; it performs no template lookup, body synthesis, parser replay or
semantic-tree construction. The new default copy is a formation-owned AST slab
copy, not a textual replay or an indirection on each body read. Semantic graphs,
patterns, source links and memos remain unit-owned; query overlays and scratch
have their existing narrow lifetimes. Lowering's destructor-entry/inertness
memo remains distinct from semantic body availability and preserves first-use
ordering. Hash indexes serve identity lookup; source lists and explicit work
queues determine diagnostics and emitted order. No whole-unit retry, global
invalidation, unrelated-declaration scan or fallback lookup was introduced.

Integer widths, target wrapping, floating bits and numeric parsing are unchanged;
sizeof/decltype/default array bounds continue through the shared typed owners.
Raw probes cover char/long/reference/cv results, repeated specialization use,
copy/move distinction and diagnostics. Production tool spellings remain intact;
new Rust identifiers follow Rust conventions. No dependency, unsafe path,
Clippy suppression, mechanical file split, environment read, external phase
implementation, optimizer or register-allocator change was introduced. Typed
LowIR ownership and per-function backend lifetimes are unchanged. Source-map
responsibilities are current. Initial function-size/bool-count audit findings
were resolved through a typed transfer enum, the owning demand-policy helper
and a reusable subobject exception query; final source audit has zero findings.

### Frozen owners, typed data flow and architecture review

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the clone plan, PA14's handout and
current ledger. Clustered all five entry failures by their earliest divergence
and read the frozen C++ owners directly. The completed group translates:

- `class_templates.cpp::instantiate_definition`'s synthesized-member branch
  and `analyzer_declarations.cpp::default_definition`: a demanded member
  explicitly defaulted outside its class template uses the concrete class's
  implicit definition. `sema/template_bodies.rs` distinguishes synthesized
  completion from a copied parsed body, marks the entity in progress before
  synthesis and publishes completion once. Constructors/destructors reuse the
  existing typed subobject plans; assignments reuse their selected action
  records. The same complete entity key reuses successful facts. No body syntax
  is manufactured, copied or reparsed for these definitions.
- `analyzer_copy.cpp::defaulted_copy_facts`: `sema/construction.rs` publishes a
  finished plan separately from computing default-constructor properties.
  `copy_construction.rs` fills an already-selected `ConstructorId` in place,
  retaining the copy property's own triviality and child selections. Earlier
  expressions and variables keep valid direct IDs. The boundary worklist
  suspends a pending member constructor before marking that plan visited, then
  follows its newly published selected edges once. It never retries unrelated
  functions, constructors or classes.
- `lower/object_lowering.cpp::copies_storage`: `sema/copy_construction.rs`'s
  lowering view separates a nontrivial ABI copy from its mechanical storage
  transfer. An instantiated out-of-class defaulted copy whose selected
  subobject copies are all trivial transfers bytes. `lower/copy_construction.rs`
  and `lower/materialization.rs` consume this recorded graph without any
  definition demand or semantic lookup. `CopyAction::Constructor.written_move`
  preserves the oracle's explicit direct-move call requirement; it does not
  infer that requirement from expression spelling during lowering.
- `analyzer_expressions.cpp::new_facts` and
  `constant_evaluator.cpp::decltype_type`: `sema/scalar_expressions.rs` completes
  a class-template object's definition when its expression facts are formed,
  including a function's class result. The existing destruction check can then
  demand the actual destructor and its selected children before lowering.
  `sema/queries.rs` saves/restores a syntax-ID marker for only decltype's final
  call/type transform, following parentheses and right-hand comma operands.
  Pointer/function shells and a final call's incomplete class result remain
  separate demands. Argument calls, materialized results and other class
  expression nodes keep the C++ completion behavior.
- `analyzer_declarations.cpp::default_definition`, explicit class roots and
  `semantics_dump.cpp::schedule`: template-pattern assignments do not prepare
  concrete definition/emission boundaries. Explicit roots recognize a source
  member's available defaulted definition. Synthesized definitions with no
  parsed body retain the oracle's semantic trailing-presentation rules, and
  defaulted destructors expose their synthesized lowering body. The same
  synthesized entity can be both an explicit root and an ordinary use; both
  worklist visits observe its completed state.
- `lower/program_lowering.cpp::destructor_does_nothing/demand_entry`: a
  destructor needed only for a lifetime can first be queried for inertness
  before its synthesized definition is available in C++. The semantic graph
  records explicit semantic uses separately from lifetime/emission preparation;
  `lower/symbols.rs` combines that typed availability fact with its own entry
  demand set. The first inertness query remains memoized at the original
  boundary, even when later emission exposes the synthesized body. Explicit
  destructor calls retain their earlier semantic availability; a class explicit
  instantiation remains an emission root whose body becomes visible on demand.
  This preserves calls, cleanup chains and definition ordering without moving
  semantic analysis back into lowering.

The unit test proves that a selected declaration plan keeps its ID, gains its
copy actions once, keeps nontrivial ABI passing while allowing storage copy,
reuses the same property ID on repeated demand, leaves syntax storage unchanged
and leaves another completed specialization's unused member undemanded.
Declaration/body facts and source links remain translation-unit owned; query
frames and synthesis scratch remain demand scoped. There is no new dependency,
text transport, environment read, global invalidation, whole-unit scan or C++
implementation fallback. Existing optimizer, register allocator, target arithmetic and per-function
backend lifetimes are unchanged; translated lowering follows the frozen
byte and ordering decisions.

`tests/controls/defaulted-member-definition-demand.t` checks nested reference

class-template results, recursive destructor demand and an incomplete decltype

### C++ owners, completed behavior and architecture review

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the Rust clone plan and PA14 handout
and ledger. Also read PA17's handout for its owning regression lane. Consulted
the frozen implementation directly:

- `syntax/ast_clone.cpp::SyntaxCloner::decl(Template)`: a nested template keeps
  its immutable parameter clause while its declaration is copied. Rust
  `syntax/cloning.rs` now handles this form, preserving the existing retained
  member bodies, source locations, shared spelling/name identities and byte
  budget guard. No token or grammar replay is introduced.
- `sema/class_templates.cpp::link_member_patterns`, `link_member`,
  `outside_pattern_member` and `instantiate_definition`: Rust
  `sema/member_patterns.rs` pairs declarations by their copied syntax positions,
  unwraps template declarations iteratively and pairs already-copied nested
  class members using a local worklist. Namespace friend templates are excluded
  from class-member links, and self links cannot be introduced. This neither
  completes deferred classes nor requests unused function bodies.
- `sema/template_names.cpp::member_template_frame/outside_frames`: a demanded
  out-of-class member-template body receives the definition's renamed enclosing
  parameter frames, bound to the concrete enclosing class arguments, followed
  by its own argument frame. Existing bridge hiding and indexed name bindings
  are retained. Only those parameter lists are copied into overlays; no whole
  visible environment is copied or searched.
- `sema/analyzer.cpp::template_parameters`,
  `analyzer_types.cpp::default_template_argument_names` and
  `class_templates.cpp::template_default`: shared parameter syntax cannot own
  facts from one enclosing specialization. Declaration checks and uncached
  function-default formation copy fact-bearing type syntax. The latter forms
  its dependent pattern at template depth in its recorded declaration lookup
  limit before signature substitution. A declaration-owned
  `templates::DefaultArgument.bound: Option<TypeId>` retains the C++ rule for
  a nondependent class-scope default, including its earlier overload binding.
  Class and function defaults consume that same typed fact. Successful default
  results retain the existing complete `(HeadId, ordinal, canonical prefix)`
  memo keys. Failed attempts publish no successful entry.
- `sema/analyzer_expressions.cpp::member_access` and
  `analyzer_calls.cpp::member_template_arguments`: explicit type arguments on a
  member designation reuse existing signature-only specialization and deduction
  while preserving its recorded receiver and naming class. Member-call failures
  retain the call location.
- `lower/symbol_lowering.cpp::abi_type_node/own_level`: ordinary template
  parameters use their ABI ordinal (`T_`, `T0_`, ...) at every lexical depth.
  Their lexical semantic depth remains part of canonical parameter identity.
  Bound-pack level encodings are a separate unfinished capability.

`sema/template_bodies.rs` follows direct typed source links only when a copied
member-template primary has no local definition pattern. A demanded
specialization records the actual definition's syntax owner, while its ABI
signature still names its copied primary. Its body is copied and analyzed once
under the existing per-entity in-progress/completed state. Later consumers read
its own facts directly. Pattern/source links live for the translation unit;
frames and substitution scratch live for their query or body. No new whole-unit
scan, global invalidation, text transport, dependency or compiler fallback was
introduced. Optimizer, register allocator and per-function backend lifetimes
are unchanged.

The new Rust ownership test checks shared retained body syntax, distinct member
heads/scopes, distinct demanded body copies, repeated-call memoization, untouched
shared default type facts and actual out-of-class definition ownership. The
existing dependent-name limit test now uses the dependent default `Y=X`; C++
returns a member template's already-bound fixed default without entering another
instantiation. Its logical-limit and context-restoration assertions remain.

The required regression is
`pa17/tests/controls/member-template-copy-demand.t`: PA17 owns member templates,
so the new control is in that executed lane rather than expanding PA14's
handout. It checks distinct enclosing arguments, defaults, repeated calls,

### Frozen owners and completed behavior

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the clone plan, PA14 handout and
current stage ledger. Consulted the frozen C++ owners directly:

- `analyzer_types.cpp::name_prefix` and `lookup_name`: a dependent qualifier's
  intermediate template-id, and a typename-specifier's final template-id,
  require `template` and form dependent specializations. They do not look up
  an unrelated visible value with the same name.
- `template_names.cpp::dependent_template_name`, `stand_in_template`,
  `specialize_template`, `substitute_template_name`, `substitute_stand_in`,
  plus `template_packs.cpp::substitute_list`: canonical qualifier/name identity,
  undefined stand-in templates, written argument lists, and template-name
  substitution before argument substitution. The written list is still visited
  after a soft failure, so a later required instantiation can diagnose its error.
- `class_templates.cpp::class_specialization`, `template_id_binding`,
  `default_template_arguments`, `template_default` and `InstantiationContext`:
  undefined shells remain dependent even with concrete arguments; a concrete
  template supplies defaults only after lookup. Ordinary signature-substitution
  failures discard candidates; errors inside required instantiations propagate.
- `lower/symbol_lowering.cpp::class_name_node`, `name_template_id` and
  `abi_type`: a dependent specialization retains its qualifier in the ABI name
  and the argument list as written, independently of concrete default completion.

Rust `sema/dependent_templates.rs` owns a unit-local hash index from canonical
`(TypeId, NameId)` to the stand-in's `EntityId`, with reverse typed facts for
substitution and lowering. The canonical stand-in itself serves as the
otherwise unobservable template-name identity; it has no parameter list,
member scope, definition or visible binding. Existing `ClassTemplates` interns
its specializations by primary identity and canonical argument IDs. Each shell
stays dependent, including `item<int>` and `item<>`. Renamed parameter frames
share canonical identity without losing their own lookup environments.

`lookup.rs` consumes the parsed disambiguators, and existing dependent-member
facts own subsequent ordinary member names. `class_templates.rs` shares concrete
template classification, parsed type argument formation and required default
completion with ordinary template-id lookup. Its explicit `DefaultUse` selects
required versus signature substitution while using the same isolated default
context, definition lookup limit and syntax cloner. Only successful defaults
are memoized by `(HeadId, parameter position, canonical prefix)`. Ordinary
failures publish no completed default or specialization; incomplete qualifier
observations prevent a context-dependent substitution result from entering its
query memo. The shared context classifies the fixed logical instantiation limit
as a hard typed error; native byte-budget failures also propagate.

`sema/lowering.rs` exposes the recorded dependent qualifier to `lower/symbols.rs`.
ABI construction uses it directly as the template name's parent, preserving
existing ABI interning and substitution numbering. It performs no semantic
lookup or syntax replay. Optimizer, register allocator, backend lifetimes and
code-generation policies are unchanged.

Two unit tests verify canonical reuse, undefined shells, retained written
arguments, partial-substitution reuse, default completion without member/body
or expression growth, hard logical limits, context restoration and a successful
retry after an incomplete qualifier's later definition. The required new
PA14 control `tests/controls/dependent-template-name-substitution.t` exercises
renamed declarations, repeated calls, concrete defaults, class-valued returns,
invalid-arity fallback and invalid-default fallback. Its protected full-course
invocation checks raw LowIR/ABI bytes as well as execution status 0. No existing
fixture, reference, fixed manifest or protected policy was changed.

### Finding, correction and required regression

The multidimensional aggregate member-array path applied its row-prefix policy
only when the address path had the standalone `ArrayStorage` root. C++
`aggregate_lowering.cpp::aggregate_elements` applies the same
`creates_temporaries` policy to counted member arrays. For a local aggregate
containing `Element cells[2][2]`, scalar converting initializers produced one
Rust prefix counter and one exception region where C++ produced a parent
counter, a reused row counter and separate row cleanup chains. Both compilers
accepted the input; generated LowIR bytes differed. This finding was not
waived because the fixed-manifest count was unchanged.

`lower/aggregates.rs::named_contents` now selects rows from the recorded array
type and initializer-temporary facts for either address root. Existing
`aggregate_arrays.rs` owns the counters and row cleanup scopes. No new scan,
syntax copy, cache or semantic query was introduced. The source map records
the corrected responsibility. `finding.json`, `row-cleanup-before.diff` and
`mismatches/4b1f1dad77844eb4ad44d05076a9df36` preserve the original difference.
The same probe matches after correction. Focused variants also match for three
dimensions, brace elision, omitted elements, nonthrowing constructors,
constructor member initialization and nested aggregates.

Required regression
`pa11/tests/controls/aggregate-member-array-row-cleanup.t` belongs to PA11's
existing object/aggregate lane. It checks element construction and reverse
destruction order; its protected full-course invocation also checks the exact
LowIR bytes, including counters and exception blocks. It passes with execution
status 0. `regression/` archives the source, raw matching receipt, generated
control outcomes and the successful PA11 course result. No existing fixture,
reference, manifest or protected gate policy changed.

### Frozen owners, typed flow and architecture review

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the clone plan, PA14 handout and
ledger, and the PA11 handout for the regression's owning lane. Consulted the
frozen C++ implementation directly:

- Initializer demand: `analyzer_classes.cpp::finish_class_nesting` and
  `analyzer_construction.cpp::member_plan`, `instantiate_member_initializer`,
  `member_default_initializer`. Rust `member_initializers.rs` stores compact
  member/binding/scope identities, immutable definition limits and explicit
  pending/in-progress/complete states. `declarations.rs` finishes pattern and
  ordinary initializers before deferred defaults/bodies. Concrete defaults are
  requested by the selected constructor paths in `construction.rs` and
  `member_construction.rs`; overrides and inactive variants bypass them.
  Complete-class lookup and `this` use the original owner. Successful demands
  reuse their typed initializer and constructor facts without syntax growth.
- Member enum definitions: `analyzer_classes.cpp::qualified_enum_definition`,
  `class_templates.cpp::declare_member_enum_instance`, `instantiate_member_enum`
  and class completion, plus `template_names.cpp::outside_frames`. Rust
  `enum_instances.rs` keeps definition and instance facts separately, with
  per-pattern waiters and per-owner pending queues. Definition events drain
  the C++ linked-list order; owner completion drains only that owner's work.
  Renamed parameter frames bind canonical arguments over the correct scoped
  enum/class lookup scope. `enumerations.rs` retains publication, first
  declaration and display ownership separately. The syntax cloner copies only
  retained enum value nodes; dump rendering is a borrowed syntax view.
- Type arguments and elaborated specializations: C++ parser name/specifier
  classification, `class_templates.cpp::template_argument`,
  `analyzer_classes.cpp::elaborated_class`, and ordinary type/function lookup.
  Rust `template_names.rs::template_type_argument` resolves the existing
  type-id/id-expression reading to a canonical `TypeId`, shared by class and
  explicit function argument formation. `class_templates.rs` specializes the
  filtered elaborated binding, rejects alias templates and validates class
  keys before recording the concrete binding. `friendships.rs` grants only the
  named specialization. Shell formation does not demand definitions or unused
  member bodies; partial/non-type/dependent-template boundaries remain explicit.
- Aggregate cleanup: `object_lowering.cpp::call_conversion`/`call_special`,
  `aggregate_lowering.cpp::aggregate_item`, `aggregate_elements`, `push_counted`,
  `begin_row`/`end_row`, and `cleanup_lowering.cpp::creates_temporaries` and
  region splitting. Rust reads selected constructor/conversion facts directly;
  destination conversions remain distinct from actual operand temporaries.
  Region starts follow subobject addressing and zero-prefix stores. Counter,
  cleanup-chain and LowIR builder state have per-function lifetimes.

Failure-path review traced both ordinary and required instantiation errors.
Instantiation contexts restore caller lookup limits, depth, access, body,
default-argument scope and dependence observations on Result returns. Failed
queries do not publish successful query state or cache negative results under
an incomplete key. Enum value failure is a terminal required-instantiation
error; partial enum bindings/syntax die with that failed unit, rather than
being used as a successful retry. The existing initializer and enum unit
checks exercise demand isolation and successful query reuse; initializer and
shared-context tests also check restoration on error. New differential probes
check both enum event orders, re-entry, the first failing waiter, complete-class
lookup and definition-time overload binding.

Sequential enum values, representability and target conversion remain in the
owning integral algorithms. Probes cover unsigned 64-bit maximum values and
underlying-type overflow rejection, as well as exact alias/key/auto diagnostics
and their source positions. Source order, waiter order and explicit output
ordering remain separate from hash-table identity. No whole-unit retry,
unrelated registry scan, copied visible environment, duplicate parser,
source/LowIR text transport, fixture shortcut, oracle delegation, unsafe code,
new dependency, blanket lint suppression or mechanical split was found in the
reviewed changes. Production CLI names stay compatible and Rust names follow
Rust conventions. Optimizer, register allocator and backend are unchanged.

## Semantic type arguments and elaborated specialization group — 2026-10-16

Implementation entry HEAD: `18a1010ddf8bc4db56a32c38d5f0f59fb1c7bb05` (clean).
The previous goal turn made progress: its committed member-enum group retained
407/418 passing cases. This group reaches **410/418**, retaining every one of
those **407** passing identities in the unchanged fixed manifest. The stage
base and last-audited commit above remain unchanged. Implementation does not
advance the audit marker. This is a coherent partial PA14 handoff for Ralph's
checkpoint review, not a claim of full-stage completion.

### Frozen owners, behavior and typed data flow

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the clone plan, PA14 handout and
current ledger. Inspected the frozen C++ sources directly:

- `syntax/parser_names.cpp::parse_template_argument` and
  `parser_specifiers.cpp::parse_named_type_specifier` retain syntactic readings
  based on parser categories. A concrete qualified name may consequently remain
  an id-expression rather than a type-id. Changing the grammar's classification
  is not the missing operation.
- `sema/class_templates.cpp::template_argument` resolves that retained
  id-expression, accepts type/alias bindings, keeps unmarked dependent qualified
  members as values, and rejects `auto`. `template_arguments` supplies these
  facts to specialization formation and canonical argument/default caches.
- `sema/analyzer_classes.cpp::elaborated_class` treats a final class template-id
  as a specialization, rejects alias-template specializations and inappropriate
  lone declarations, checks its union/class key, and grants friendship to the
  selected specialization. This does not require its definition.
- `sema/analyzer_types.cpp::lookup_name` keeps elaborated lookup filtering and
  final specialization separate. `function_templates.cpp` and
  `analyzer_expressions.cpp` consume the same argument classification.

Rust `template_names.rs::template_type_argument` returns a typed
`Result<Option<TypeId>>` over the retained `TypeId` or `ExprId`/`Name` syntax.
Only existing syntax is inspected; successful name lookup records its
`BindingId`, and callers canonicalize the resulting type in their existing
specialization owner. Class formation, explicit function calls/addresses and
function-template definition-name checking now share this operation. Dependent
qualified members without the disambiguator retain the existing unimplemented
non-type boundary; they are not silently reclassified as concrete types.
Function argument formation receives the component's `SourceOffset` explicitly,
so an argument's nested lookup cannot move a later `auto` diagnostic.

`class_templates.rs::elaborated_specialization` performs filtered lookup without
specializing the final component until alias-template exclusion, then invokes
ordinary class specialization/default formation and class-key validation.
`resolved_names` records the specialization binding rather than its primary.
`declarations.rs` dispatches final template-ids before its ordinary forward/tag
path; an active partial-specialization head retains status 86 rather than
being language-rejected by that ordinary path. `friendships.rs` records the
specific specialization edge and excludes a friend partial-specialization
declaration. Ordinary tag lookup remains with
its owning path. The source map records these translated responsibilities.

Architecture review: parse once; immutable source and unit-owned syntax slabs;
compact names, bindings, entities and canonical types; no token/source replay,
second semantic tree, whole-program scan, retry pass or new owning cache.
Specialization/default identity, lookup limits, class completion states and
per-member/body demand remain in their existing owners. Qualified type lookup
still completes the owners whose members it must find; merely forming a
resulting class shell does not complete that specialization or request unused
bodies. The new unit checks prove that expression/type readings share one
hidden concrete shell with an invalid
unused definition, preserve the original expression reading, and grant only the
named friend specialization while both friend shells stay incomplete. No
optimizer, allocator, backend, stack guard, dependency, environment read,
compiler fallback, fixture or protected policy change was introduced.

### Frozen owners, completed behavior and typed data flow

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the clone plan, PA14 handout and
current ledger, and inspected the frozen C++ owners directly:

- `analyzer_classes.cpp::qualified_enum_definition` retains a member enum's
  parsed definition, checks pattern values through a qualified lookup bridge,
  and completes already waiting instances once the definition is analyzed.
  Scoped definitions print their written qualified name; unscoped enumerators
  retain their first declaration's class ownership.
- `class_templates.cpp::qualifying_template`, `declare_member_enum_instance`,
  `instantiate_member_enum` and `instantiate_class` identify the qualified
  template owner, link opaque instance enums to their pattern and enclosing
  specialization, and finish pending enums after their owner's class body and
  member-pattern links. Already complete owners can instantiate immediately.
- `template_names.cpp::outside_frames` binds definition parameter names over
  the concrete class or enum lookup scope. The enum instantiator passes no
  filtering bridge: its original parameter names remain bound even when a
  definition-time member lookup bridge omitted a hidden name. This observable
  distinction is retained, rather than corrected to a different language rule.
- `syntax/ast_clone.cpp` copies only enumerators and their value expressions;
  `syntax/ast_render.cpp::RenderName` preserves template arguments in written
  qualified enum labels.

Rust `enum_instances.rs` owns private sparse stores keyed by compact enum
`EntityId`. A pattern holds its immutable definition `DeclId`, optional
`HeadId`, original parameter `ScopeId` and namespace lookup limit. A concrete
instance holds its source enum, enclosing class ID and explicit
`Pending`/`InProgress`/`Complete` query state. Repeated opaque declarations do
not enqueue duplicates. Waiting instances are indexed by pattern definition;
completion work is indexed by its owning class. Definition events consume
only their own waiters, in C++ linked-list order; class completion consumes
only that owner's pending entries. There is no whole-unit retry scan.

`member_patterns.rs` includes enum names in preliminary owner discovery and
separates the enclosing argument owner from the lookup base in
`outside_lookup_frame`. The existing function/member callers keep their
original class lookup base. `enumerations.rs` checks qualified placement in
the enclosing declaration scope and keeps enumerator publication, value
lookup, print scope and first declaration identity distinct. Concrete queries
bind renamed canonical type arguments, copy just the retained value syntax,
then evaluate through `required_instantiation` with the owner's formation
depth and the definition's lookup ordinal. Caller body, access, default scope,
dependence observations and lookup state are restored on Result paths.
Successful enum queries reuse typed constant facts without new syntax or
bindings. Sequential values and target range/overflow conversions continue to
use the existing owning enumeration/integral algorithms.

`dump.rs` now uses the existing iterative retained-syntax name renderer for
written enum labels, preserving `A<U>::E` in types, scopes and enumerator type
labels. Rendering remains a borrowed presentation view. The source map records
all these responsibilities.

Architecture review: parse once; unit-owned syntax slabs and typed identities;
no source/token replay or second semantic tree. Sparse query lookup is average
O(1), and event drains are linear in their own queued instances. Only demanded
class instances acquire concrete enum values. Lowering reads the existing
constant/entity facts. No optimizer, register allocator, backend, guard,
dependency, environment read, fallback compiler, protected input or policy
change was introduced. The new unit test checks both definition-event orders,
scoped/unscoped instance ownership, empty event queues and completed-query
reuse with unchanged syntax/binding counts and caller dependence state.

  demanded defaults retain definition-context lookup and complete-class `this`.

  converting construction does not perform the ordinary constructor-call

and inactive union variants do not request their unused defaults. Late class
members and explicit `this` now resolve in the completed class scope. The
frozen lazy-initializer control also proves that a later namespace overload

Rust `lower/construction.rs` distinguishes the selected destination conversion

preserving exact marker placement and the existing construction/destruction
order for aggregate members and class-member arrays. Actual operand temporaries
still receive their own cleanup regions. The source map records these owners.

Architecture review: the source is parsed once; class instantiation continues
to use its existing fact-bearing syntax copy, with no new token/text replay or
second semantic tree. Query identity is a compact member ID whose declaring
scope/limit remain immutable. Successful-query lookup is average O(1); the
class-completion drain is linear in its owned deferred entries and has no
translation-unit scan or global invalidation. Only constructors using defaults
request concrete initializer facts. Lowering consumes retained facts and its
existing per-function cleanup state. Optimizer, register allocator and backend
are unchanged. No unsafe, dependency, environment read, compiler fallback,
reference answer or protected-policy change was introduced.

## Independent audit of the accepted enum, re-entry and ADL groups — 2026-10-16

Audit entry HEAD: `cd2072ac` (clean). Previous goal turn: progress, evidenced by
that committed ADL group and its retained 404/418 result. Reviewed the complete
implementation/source-map/fixture diff from `29d1d2f5` through the three accepted
checkpoints `482646f0`, `93d9a8e3` and `cd2072ac`, then the corrective
implementation `ab55374292610a3a700423e1d5c277db478b7b05`. The last-audited
marker now names that reviewed implementation. Read AGENTS.md, the clone
contract, spec, clone plan, PA14 handout and ledger; consulted frozen C++ owners
directly. This certifies only those partial groups, not full PA14 completion.

### Findings, corrections and regressions

1. **Retained operand substitution leaked dependence into its demander.**
   `template_operands.rs::evaluate_operand` restored its body, scope, lookup,
   access and initialization context but omitted the new dependent-use counter.
   Substituting `decltype(X(), N::Arg())` for a fixed `make(0)` marked the outer
   expression dependent. Consequently `pick(make(0))` inside an enclosing
   template incorrectly found a later, better associated overload. The first
   call and a subsequent cached call could bind differently. Failed operands
   could leak observations too. C++ `template_placeholders.cpp::substituted_value`
   evaluates through `class_templates.cpp::InstantiationContext`, whose
   destructor restores those observations. The Rust operand query now saves,
   clears and restores the counter on success and every Result error path.
   It retains the operand's own dependence facts and its existing transient
   incomplete-class and active-key behavior.
2. **Selected default-argument instantiation had the same omission.**
   `template_defaults.rs::instantiate_default` let names from `X()` in an omitted
   default mark a fixed outer call dependent, admitting later associated
   overloads on the first demand. C++ `class_templates.cpp::instantiate_default_argument`
   uses its independent instantiation context. Rust now isolates and restores
   the counter before publishing the selected parameter's completed default.

New executed PA14 controls:

- `tests/controls/300-adl-operand-substitution-context-isolation.t`: successful
  and failed operands, repeated queries, explicit template-ids, operator ADL,
  and a fixed comma-expression result.
- `tests/controls/300-adl-default-instantiation-context-isolation.t`: first and
  repeated omitted-default use, class/scalar template arguments, operator ADL
  and a fixed comma-expression result.

Existing Rust failure-path tests now seed a nonzero caller counter and verify
its exact restoration. The operand test observes a dependent name before
failure and checks empty active membership and no completed failed result.
Pre-fix scratch mismatches remain under the evidence root's `mismatches/`;
`reentry-probes.log` and the protected fixture receipts show the fixes.

### Frozen owners, typed flow and architecture review

| Responsibility | Frozen C++ owners | Reviewed Rust owners |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Enum integral operand typing, exact query values and conversion context | `analyzer_classes.cpp`, `analyzer_constexpr.cpp::integral_constant_operand`, `analyzer_expressions.cpp::size_of/SpecializationType`, `lower/expression_lowering.cpp::IsSizeQuery/assignment` | `enumerations.rs`, `constants.rs`, `queries.rs`, `expression_facts.rs`, `lowering.rs`, `lower/expressions.rs`, `lower/mutations.rs` |
| Class-aware signature recursion and transient operand results | `function_templates.cpp::specialize/ActiveSpecialization`, `class_templates.cpp::complete_class`, `template_placeholders.cpp::substituted_value` | `function_templates.rs`, `instance_definitions.rs`, `template_operands.rs`, `analyzer.rs` |
| Binding/expression provenance, call/operator association contexts | `lookup.cpp::unqualified/DependentClassScope`, `analyzer_access.cpp::declare_in`, `analyzer_declarations.cpp`, `analyzer_bodies.cpp`, `analyzer_calls.cpp::call/argument_dependent`, `analyzer_operators.cpp` | `graph.rs`, `lookup.rs`, `dependence.rs`, `declarations.rs`, `specifiers.rs`, `statements.rs`, `scalar_expressions.rs`, `procedural_calls.rs`, `call_lookup.rs`, `operator_candidates.rs`, `template_context.rs`, `template_defaults.rs` |

Source is parsed once. Demanded copies own their fact-bearing AST IDs and share
immutable spelling/source storage. The private dense BindingId dependence
column is initialized at the graph's sole binding insertion path; parameter
provenance follows only indexed primary/member source edges. Expression
provenance and query kind are small typed facts, exported directly to lowering.
Parentheses share facts, binary spines are iterative, and fixed-result queries
restore their incoming observation count. Explicit arguments are formed once
as canonical TypeIds and reused after argument typing and association.

Completed signature/operand maps use canonical entity/argument/context keys.
Active membership additionally distinguishes active class-definition depth;
cleanup runs before returning errors. Nested successful signatures retain one
BindingId. Incomplete-class observations prevent publication of transient
operand and local substitution results, without invalidating unrelated caches.
Class completion, signature, default, exception, individual body, storage and
emission demands remain distinct. The two fixes neither broaden body demand
nor retain extra syntax copies.

Associated lookup follows argument types, required bases/enclosing classes,
indexed inline namespace edges and the requested name's function/friend sets.
Its context limits functions, while ordinary lookup keeps its definition limit.
Instance friends preserve the frozen visibility exception. Scratch sets are
reclaimed with capacity retained; deduplication and stable candidate insertion

### Direct source owners and completed behavior

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the PA14 handout, clone plan and
current ledger. Consulted the frozen C++ owners directly under
`/home/vishvananda/work/references/v5rust-cpp/dev/src/`:

| C++ owner | Rust owner and responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `sema/function_templates.cpp::specialize`, `ActiveSpecialization` | `function_templates.rs`: completed signatures retain the canonical primary/argument key. Separate active membership also records class-definition depth. Re-entering from a newly started class definition can form a signature from members already declared there; recursion at the same depth remains a failed substitution. An outer formation reuses the nested `BindingId`, preserving one entity and its declaration facts. |
| `sema/class_templates.cpp::instantiate_class`, `complete_class`; `function_templates.cpp::substitute_member` | `instance_definitions.rs`, `analyzer.rs`: a private unit-owned stack of active class `EntityId`s brackets class-body analysis and is restored on failure. Incomplete completion queries record a checked observation counter. The existing dependent-member query exposes the class's declared members without restarting its definition. |
| `sema/template_placeholders.cpp::substituted_value` | `template_operands.rs`, `function_templates.rs`: type/bound operand completions are separate from depth-qualified active queries. A successful evaluation that observed an incomplete class remains transient, including local type-DAG memo results. Later declarations can therefore change the next trial's result. Failed trials remove only their active membership and preserve nested completions. |

The original two failures shared the signature guard: every in-progress key
previously failed, even after starting a class definition that had declared its
required typedef. The C++ owner explicitly distinguishes those contexts. Source
review and a trailing-result probe also found the same overly broad guard on
operand substitution. A probe with equivalent `decltype` operands before/after
the typedef established why successful incomplete-class trials must not be
published. This implements the production invariants as well as the two gains.

manifest or any existing fixture/reference:

  later typed result from the same operand, repeated function address identity.

completed specialization/entity, empty active state and no body demand from

### Typed flow, demand and architecture review

Syntax is still parsed once. Existing class/operand cloners supply the demanded
instance's AST IDs; this change adds no source/token replay or owning semantic
tree. Canonical `EntityId`/`TypeId` keys and selected `BindingId`s retain identity.

query-local membership by the complete canonical key plus class-definition
depth; active class storage retains only compact IDs. All active paths restore
state before propagating `Result` errors. A nested completed signature remains
owned by its canonical key instead of being replaced by the outer call.

The incomplete-observation counter only determines whether the current query
may publish its result. It clears no cache, schedules no retry, scans no registry
and changes no ordinary lookup limit. Re-evaluation of an intentionally transient
operand is required by changing class-member availability. Completed keys retain
ordinary constant-time lookup. Signature completion, class completion, each
body/default/exception, storage and emission remain separate demands. In
particular, unevaluated re-entry does not demand a function body.

Lowering receives the existing typed selected calls and types. No optimizer,
register allocator, code generator, target numeric algorithm, emission order or
per-function backend lifetime changed. No dependency, unsafe/FFI, compiler
subprocess, environment read, lint suppression or mechanical file split was
introduced. `source-map.tsv` records these responsibilities; the companion file

### Completed behavior and direct source owners

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the PA14 handout, clone plan and
current ledger. Consulted the frozen C++ source directly under
`/home/vishvananda/work/references/v5rust-cpp/dev/src/`:

| C++ owner | Rust owner and translated responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `sema/analyzer_classes.cpp::enumerators`, `analyzer_constexpr.cpp::integral_constant_operand` | `enumerations.rs`, `constants.rs`: concrete enum values undergo normal expression typing and function resolution before constant evaluation. A fixed underlying target selects the existing scalar conversion for a class operand; otherwise the existing unique implicit integral/unscoped-enum conversion is used. Definition-time template name checks remain separate from concrete cloned facts. |
| `sema/analyzer_expressions.cpp::SpecializationType`, `size_of` and alignof dispatch | `queries.rs`, `expression_facts.rs`: a dense `QueryKind` enum records direct class-specialization provenance beside the cached size/alignment value. `sizeof` tests the type after reference removal; `alignof` tests only an explicit type-id, before stripping references/arrays. cv and aliases retain canonical identity; compound types and nested member instances remain distinct. |
| `lower/expression_lowering.cpp::IsSizeQuery/arithmetic/assignment`, `conversion_lowering.cpp::WideningFolds` | `lower/expressions.rs`, `mutations.rs`, `sema/lowering.rs`: additive operands beside specialization queries use the ordinary additive conversion context. Only non-static members declared by a specialization use initialization-style literal widening on plain assignment. Static, ordinary/inherited ordinary, nested member-instance and braced assignments retain their existing contexts. |

Raw evidence ruled out an exception-specification bug in
`400-unwind-template-noexcept-sizeof`: `unwind=no` was already correct; the
first divergence was the literal conversion assigning `wide.value = 2`.
The query flag likewise explains the explicit sign extension beside
`sizeof(Pad<D>)` in the frozen output. In the other two gains, typing enum
initializers resolves the overload in `sizeof(call(...))` and retains its
unevaluated facts; the integral evaluator no longer reaches the incomplete
untyped-call query. No static reference definition, initializer function body,
or storage is demanded by the unevaluated use.

Three new executed regressions have new oracle-derived references; no existing
fixture or reference changed:

- PA7 `tests/general/300-enumerator-sizeof-overload-call.t`: overloads selected
  by int/char arguments within enum queries, and a later enum-value dependency.
- PA14 `tests/general/300-specialization-size-query-conversion-context.t`:
  cv/alias, reference, array and expression operands for size/alignment queries.
- PA14 `tests/general/300-specialization-member-assignment-conversion-context.t`:
  declaring-owner policy across specialization, ordinary base, ordinary derived,
  nested member instance and static/braced assignments, including a member body.

### Ownership, order and architecture review

Syntax is parsed once. Concrete enum initializers use the instance's existing
cloned AST IDs; no source/token replay or owning semantic-tree copy is added.
Typing records selected bindings, calls and conversions before the constant
interpreter reads the cached query value. The existing `unevaluated` context
continues to distinguish operand typing from function/storage demand. Template
patterns keep their separate name/fixed-expression walk.

`QueryKind` is a small enum on the dense expression fact and its typed lowering
view. Canonical `TypeId`, class `EntityId` and selected `BindingId` determine
specialization and declaring-owner identity. Lowering performs no lookup,
retyping, specialization scan, global retry or invalidation. The new member
query follows only the selected member's owner. Evaluation still computes both
arithmetic values before applying their conversions, and assignment still
evaluates the right operand before the destination address. Conversion opcodes,
widths, truncation/sign-extension and output presentation remain the frozen
algorithms; this change selects their existing contexts.

No optimizer/register-allocator/backend policy, function lifetime, emission
scheduler or target numeric algorithm changed. No dependency, unsafe/FFI,
compiler subprocess, environment access, mechanical file split or lint
suppression was introduced. `source-map.tsv` records each responsibility;
the independent file audit reports 287 files and zero findings.

### Ownership, failure, demand and order review

`NameComponent` and parsed type-argument nodes remain the only syntax. The
`BaseDependency` fact is recorded at definition time and retained by
`syntax/cloning.rs::base`, whose node copy only replaces its `Name` ID. Concrete
base edges retain provenance independently of their now-concrete type. No
parser, token/source replay or second whole semantic tree was added.

Scope/name indexes, canonical `EntityId`/`TypeId` specialization keys and
`BindingId` imports preserve identity. Unqualified base queries skip only direct
dependent edges of the queried class; fixed-base subtrees use qualified lookup.
The representative, ambiguity and same-primary injected-name facts remain in
compact query-local base memos. Ordinary names reject ambiguity; explicit
arguments recover only actual injected names of the same primary, never alias
lookalikes. Lookup visits named lexical/base/namespace edges, not an unrelated
registry; no global scan, persistent incomplete-key cache or invalidation was
introduced. Prefix access follows source component order and uses its actual
locations. Complete overload-set access checks precede import hiding.

Explicit class roots select only own, already-defined patterns in source binding
order, recurse through required nested classes, retain canonical extern/definition
state and preserve inline/excluded/always-inline exceptions. Signature/class
completion, individual bodies/defaults/exceptions, static initializers, storage
and emission stay separate demands. Required-instantiation contexts restore
caller state on failure; class/body/property owners retain their existing
in-progress/complete memos. The new copy path distinguishes a known deleted
property from a missing required semantic fact; it does not hide failed
selection in an ordinary use. Unused invalid bodies remain uninstantiated.

Member qualifiers resolve explicit arguments in expression context and require
the object class or a base. Destructors retain canonical selected bindings,
receiver cv/category, naming/base path and dispatch. Lowering consumes these
facts and the recorded plans; it does not redo lookup or selection. Explicit
function/constructor roots, class tables and remaining static variables retain
the frozen demand and presentation order. Exact raw LowIR probes cover source
order, ABI aliases, object metadata, inline flags and storage-copy sizes.
No target numeric algorithm changed. The backend, optimizer, register allocator
and per-function ownership were not changed. No unsafe/FFI, oracle delegation,
new dependency, environment read, mechanical source split or lint suppression
was introduced. Source-map responsibilities and the direct-member-function
boundary are current; the source audit reports zero findings.

### Frozen owners, behavior and architecture review

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the PA14 handout, clone plan and
current ledger. Consulted frozen `analyzer_expressions.cpp::member_qualifier/`
`destructor_access`, `lookup.cpp::SameTemplateInjectedNames/merge_base`,
