and one incorrect destructor-name type comparison after pointer-only use of a
class specialization. All four now match the oracle exactly.

| C++ responsibility | Rust owner and typed data flow |
| --- | --- |
| Member nested-name-specifier | `sema/member_expressions.rs`: each retained `NameComponent` resolves through object-class lookup, then expression context for the first name, or the prior prefix for later names. An explicit argument list uses the existing canonical class-template owner in the expression `ScopeId`; the resolved class must be the object or a base. Raw representative/ambiguity facts preserve C++ access-check order. |
| Explicit destructor template-id and injected class name | `sema/destructor_expressions.rs`: complete the object class before searching its injected name, specialize a written destructor template-id, compare unqualified canonical `TypeId`s, then retain the selected destructor `BindingId`, original object cv/category, naming class/base path and direct/virtual dispatch. Existing body/exception and emission APIs keep their demand boundaries. |
| Repeated injected names | `sema/lookup.rs`, `class_templates.rs`: base lookup keeps representative `ScopeId`, ambiguity and same-primary injected-template flags in compact query scratch. Only type bindings declared in their own specialization scope with the same primary `EntityId` qualify. Ordinary names still reject ambiguity; an explicit template-id can recover the common template. Aliases naming different specializations remain ambiguous. |
| Source-name checked versus raw lookup | `sema/lookup.rs`, `builtin_calls.rs`: ordinary queries check ambiguity at the use; final type names and nested-name-specifiers may recover only the injected-template case when followed by arguments. Member qualifiers/destructors consume the representative as their C++ owners do. Builtin lookup explicitly initializes the added fact. |

The parser retains one tree; no token/source replay or text transport was added.
Canonical class/default argument keys and explicit in-progress/complete class
states are reused. Base lookup still walks only indexed names/base edges with
query-local memos, retaining one compact result per scope and collecting the
representative overload set once. It copies no overload vectors per branch and
adds no persistent cache, unrelated scan, global invalidation or speculative
member-body work. Class completion, member body demand, layout and emission
remain separate. Optimizer, register allocator, code generation and per-function
backend lifetimes are unchanged. No unsafe, FFI, environment read, new dependency,
compiler fallback or lint suppression was introduced. Source-map owners are current.

Two ownership regressions verify canonical injected representatives across nested
base merges, rejection of ambiguous ordinary queries, alias lookalikes and mixed

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the PA14 handout, clone plan and
current ledger. Consulted the frozen `lookup.cpp::class_members/`
`class_members_without_dependent_bases`, `analyzer.cpp::using_declaration/introduce`,

then at concrete unqualified lookup changing definition-time name binding.

| C++ responsibility | Rust owner and typed flow |
| --- | --- |
| Definition-time base provenance, including local classes | `syntax/ast.rs`, `syntax/storage.rs`, `sema/inheritance.rs`, `graph.rs`: semantic `BaseDependency` is recorded in the original base list and retained by existing syntax cloning; concrete `BaseClass` edges carry it independently of whether the bound type is now concrete; only pattern class summaries enable unknown-specialization lookup |
| Unqualified versus qualified base lookup | `sema/lookup.rs`: a query skips dependent direct edges only at its starting class, while fixed direct bases and their inherited members use qualified traversal; existing query-local `ScopeId` memos and ambiguity merging are retained |
| Dependent using declarations | `sema/declarations.rs`, `lookup.rs`, existing `dependent_members.rs`: a typed scope/dependent-qualifier enum preserves the distinction; a dependent identifier introduces the interned qualifier/member placeholder only if the scope lacks that name; constructor names and nonidentifier names wait for the concrete copy; destructor using declarations retain the exact rejection |
| Concrete imports and own declarations | `declarations.rs`, `declarators.rs`: ordinary concrete replay reuses imported `EntityId`/`TypeId`, enum facts, overload order and complete-set access checks; own functions may join/hide a dependent placeholder; imported types and values occupy their proper separate spaces; unqualified imported type names need no typename |
| Current instantiation and unknown specializations | `lookup.rs`, `declarators.rs`: a qualified source-name query missing from a dependent-base pattern gets the canonical dependent-member identity; ordinary declaration-set lookup stays distinct; qualified dependent types keep the existing exact typename diagnostic |
| Inheriting-constructor semantic presentation | `function_demands.rs`, `semantics_dump.rs`: retained inherited-constructor identity follows C++'s implicit queue policy, rendering parameterless definitions/default-object calls and omitting parameterized implicit constructors from trailing declarations |

The parser still runs once. Base provenance is a semantic fact on the parsed
syntax, retained during the existing demanded copy, rather than re-derived
from a concrete binding or source text. Imports preserve the original member's
entity/type; no proxy scope is invented for a dependent qualifier. No second
semantic tree, speculative member-body walk, global polling/cache invalidation,
new dependency, unsafe/FFI, environment read or compiler fallback was added.
Emission, optimizer/register allocator and per-function backend ownership are
unchanged. Source-map responsibilities are current.

The ownership regression verifies original pattern flags, copied edge flags,
fixed-base independence, canonical imported identity and the absence of demand
for an unused invalid member body. Two new PA14 controls exercise type/value
imports, enumerators, overload hiding, implicit assignment, local/nested base
lookup and complete-overload-set access rejection at instantiation.

| C++ responsibility | Rust owner and typed flow |
| --- | --- |
| Explicit class/member-class declaration checks and member selection | `sema/explicit_classes.rs`, `explicit_instantiations.rs`: retained elaborated syntax to canonical `BindingId`/`EntityId`, source-pattern role, union-key and namespace placement, access-exempt lookup, class completeness, canonical extern/definition state and exact duplicate diagnostics |
| Available definitions at the explicit-instantiation point | `instance_definitions.rs`, `template_bodies.rs`, `variable_instances.rs`: source/member links and source-order bindings select only defined own members; inherited, excluded, undefined and later-defined members stay outside the explicit demand set; nested classes recursively contribute their own definitions and static data |
| Exclusion and extern suppression | `layout_attributes.rs`, `analyzer.rs`, `special_members.rs`, `explicit_classes.rs`, `lowering.rs`: sparse typed per-entity exclusion/always-inline policy includes declaration and nested declarator attributes, and consults source-pattern flags; inline/excluded/always-inline exceptions preserve implicit body demands; extern-class static initializers retain semantic facts but storage emits declarations |
| Unit-tail body/synthesized demands | `template_bodies.rs`, `construction.rs`: copied bodies reuse the existing guarded formation-depth worklist and definition-owned frames; defaulted synthesis runs through required-instantiation context and existing state checks; a defaulted member also in the ordinary queue does not repeat preparation |
| Function, table and variable roots | `sema/layout.rs`, `virtual_members.rs`, `lowering.rs`, `lower/mod.rs`: explicit function roots use their typed constructor/function entries, omit deleted roots, then demand class tables and finally the remaining static variables; table slot/layout facts use the existing boundary worklist, without memoizing an unavailable key definition as complete |

initializer, ordinary later use, copy constructor, destructor, unwind facts,
object-root metadata and ABI aliases independently. This preserves the oracle's
observable demand behavior rather than inventing an implicit initialization.

text reparsing, second semantic-tree transport or whole-program speculative
walk. Required work visits the named class's own ordered bindings and required
nested-class edges. Canonical state/policy and source-member links use compact
IDs and direct indexes. Existing definition/initializer/body memos retain
in-progress and complete states; failed required contexts restore their caller.
Emission remains separate from semantic completion, and each function keeps
its existing transient LowIR/backend lifetime. No unsafe, FFI, dependency,
environment read, compiler fallback, optimizer/register-allocator change or
lint suppression was introduced. Source map responsibilities are current.

body IDs, two static definitions are demanded once despite repeated references,
and excluded/undefined/later definitions remain uninstantiated. New controls
exercise recursive static startup, source-point selection, excluded declaration

## Independent checkpoint audit of accepted PA14 groups — 2026-10-16

Audit entry HEAD: `77a284acd2a6aa73abaeb82d0adf70e63a070d91` (clean).
Reviewed implementation range: `8c257210fe773e5127f490701577c9151f8538de..77a284acd2a6aa73abaeb82d0adf70e63a070d91`,
then the corrective implementation commit `ec74e8747e3c641f1da1ef71838dbda3729149d7`.
The previous interrupted turn supplied no verified implementation result; this
turn inspected the authoritative clean checkout and established a fresh entry
receipt. Ralph's state, goals, protected checker policy, frozen fixtures and
C++ reference remain unchanged.

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the handout, clone plan, stage
ledger and source map. This audit certifies the accepted demanded-class,
qualified-member-frame/body and static-member-initializer/storage groups.
It does not require the remaining PA14 groups to pass or advance the stage.

### Findings and corrections

1. **Friend patterns leaked namespace identity.** The template's own ordinary
   friend declaration could redeclare an existing namespace function, or enter
   its overload/ADL indexes before any class instance existed. A definition
   then reused dependent pattern facts; a declaration could change a strong
   function into a weak demanded member. `declarations.rs`, `graph.rs` and
   `function_names.rs` now retain separate source-facing friend pattern
   bindings without namespace or ADL publication. Each concrete class declares
   the actual namespace function and records only its own hidden-friend edge.
2. **Member links captured unrelated functions.** Positional member linking
   unconditionally linked namespace friends and already-selected function
   template specializations. In particular, a friend `f<>(int)` caused the
   actual free-function specialization's body to disappear. `member_patterns.rs`
   now excludes own templates, existing specializations and ordinary namespace
   friends. An inline friend defined in an instance retains its namespace home
   and its class-owned lexical definition frame; `template_bodies.rs` defers
   and copies only its demanded body, independently of other instance bodies.
3. **Declaration agreement used expression visibility.** During required class
   instantiation, the definition's lookup ordinal hid a namespace function
   already declared at the instantiation site, creating another entity with
   the same signature and an ambiguous call. The private `redeclared_value`
   API and namespace signature index now agree against already-introduced
   declarations directly. Ordinary lookup still applies its definition limit;
   no global lookup-limit reset or unrelated declaration scan was introduced.
4. **Object completion published the variable too early.** When completion
   declares namespace friends, the source type view placed those declarations
   after the variable instead of before it. `ValueScope` carries the pending
   object-initialization check, so completeness/abstract checks run before the
   binding is published. The ordinary initializer and entity owners remain
   unchanged. The semantic ownership test checks the resulting order directly.
5. **Nested dynamic initialization ran too late.** An initializer referencing a
   newly demanded member finished before the referenced initializer ran. A
   cyclic `first = second + 1; second = first + 2` consequently computed
   different values, as well as different LowIR. `startup.rs` now moves the
   active builder into the same startup owner while referenced definitions
   run at their first evaluated reference. Body, block, slot and temporary IDs
   are preserved, and the caller resumes with the updated block/exception
   numbering. Each nested initializer has its own scratch and temporary owner.
   Already-published symbols whose definitions remain in a suspended sibling
   queue are completed at the reference too; their stale queue entries observe
   completed storage and perform no second initialization. `globals.rs` checks
   the shared unit byte-budget guard on recursive definition entry.

These are general ownership/scheduling corrections, not fixture-specific
exceptions. The fixed-manifest count was unchanged; that did not waive any
finding. New executed PA14 controls are:

- `tests/controls/300-audit-friend-pattern-namespace-identity.t`
- `tests/controls/300-audit-friend-instance-signature-agreement.t`
- `tests/controls/300-audit-friend-body-demand.t`
- `tests/controls/300-audit-static-member-initializer-reference-order.t`

| `sema/analyzer_declarations.cpp::declare_function/earlier_function/define_function/declare_variable`, `lookup.cpp::find/overload` | `declarations.rs`, `function_names.rs`, `graph.rs`: separate friend patterns, canonical signature agreement and declaration publication; indexed declaration agreement is separate from expression visibility |

Traced declarations and demanded templates from the single parsed syntax into
canonical TypeId/EntityId facts, through class/layout/member demand and typed
LowIR. Source patterns retain their syntax; no text is reparsed and no second
semantic tree is transported. Names/types/entities are compact IDs; strings
remain presentation or ABI data. Declaration lookup and instantiation memo
queries use direct indexes, not global scans. Copies are limited to demanded
fact-bearing syntax and compact argument lists, not whole visible environments
or IR bodies. `take_construction` transfers the body and slot-name owner without
cloning them; only an inert builder placeholder remains during nested work.
On a failing nested initializer the Result aborts compilation and never
publishes a successful function; required semantic contexts restore callers
and clear unsuccessful completion memos as before.

The new semantic unit invariant checks four friend links across two canonical
class instances, exactly two unique demanded body copies despite repeated
calls, unused bad bodies remaining uninstantiated, source-pattern identities
outside namespace overloads, namespace ownership with instance lexical frames,
and friend-before-variable publication. Existing tests exercise failed class
context restoration, repeated successful definition/initializer reuse,
independent nested definitions, incremental vtable/body boundaries and delayed
emission child ordering. No new Clippy suppression, unsafe code, dependency,
compiler subprocess fallback, environment read, duplicate parser, mechanical
split, optimization or register-allocation change was introduced. Production
tool names and Rust naming conventions remain compatible. The source map
records the corrected responsibilities.

### Completed invariants and architecture review

Variable declarations compare canonical identities, including the frozen rule
allowing an omitted major array bound, while source-facing bindings keep their
own declared spelling. Qualified pattern definitions retain their own renamed
heads and declaration ordinal. They never enqueue source initializer syntax
for ordinary image evaluation. Their declaration legality is still checked,
including unused bad types, duplicate definitions and repeated in-class
initializers.

Class completion links each static member to its source by the existing
ordered declarator correspondence. A use obtains the selected canonical
member through ordinary indexed lookup. Unknown array bounds and unknown
integral constant values can require its initializer immediately, even when
storage remains unevaluated. A separate deduplicated queue records evaluated
storage uses, including folded constant reads in the course's form. Missing
definitions remain ordinary weak declarations; later source definitions can
satisfy a later demand. No missing implementation exit is treated as rejection.

A demanded initializer copies only its fact-bearing parsed expressions and
checks them once in the definition's bound frame, with required-instantiation
context restoration and lookup limit. The existing initializer, constant,
array-image, constructor/destructor and storage-layout owners compute concrete
facts. Out-of-class storage definitions retain an existing in-class value.
Completion of a class never demands an unused initializer. Nested ordinary
class members use the same positional source links. Explicit static-member
instantiation reuses this fact owner and does not broaden to class instantiation.

Lowering reads those facts. Symbol publication schedules each instance once.
Independent pending variable queues are suspended/restored by moving their
owners, not by copying the sibling queue. A flat parent-linked request store
lets delayed initializer calls occupy their original reference position;
append/traversal is linear in requests and never shifts or rescans unrelated
function work. Queue indices are private host storage offsets, while semantic
relationships use the existing compact entity/type/syntax IDs. No function is
lowered twice; its builder and backend lifetime remain per function.

Dynamic weak initialization sets the shared ABI guard before running the
initializer and sets this unit's constructed flag after successful construction.
Its finalizer tests that unit flag. Reference temporary origin is independent
of the flag: unrolled member arrays take each element's address separately,
while reference temporary arrays retain the frozen shared-base form.
Optimization and register allocation are unchanged; no unsafe, FFI/compiler
fallback, environment reads, text reparsing, fixture recognition or new
optimization was introduced. Protected checks, fixtures, references, oracle
sources and Ralph state remain unchanged. The source map records each owner.

The semantic ownership unit test proves three class specializations/six member
links, only two demanded initializers, deduplicated repeated use, fresh copied
expression IDs, retained untyped source patterns and an unevaluated invalid
member remaining uninstantiated. The ordering unit test proves nested delayed
children precede siblings and new roots remain visible after queue exhaustion.
The test-only phase dependency discovered by the companion audit was removed;
the ordering test now tests the value-independent queue directly.

### Completed invariants and architecture review

The owner probe follows the declared name's qualifier scopes, leaving argument
validation to the ordinary declaration path. Ambiguous preliminary lookup
stops the probe and preserves the real diagnostic's location. Required
instantiation swaps/restores the current owner together with all existing
caller context. The owning class template's own arguments denote its current
instantiation in member decl-specifiers as well as the declarator and body.
Canonical parameter comparison computes the canonical fact when needed; it
never depends on a previous signature having visited that type.

Qualified member definitions retain their own `HeadId` and visible-name bridge,
separate from the instantiated member's entity template head. Class and fixed
base members hide enclosing-head names; member-template heads remain distinct.
Each bound frame contains only that declaration's retained parameter names,
with types read positionally from the canonical enclosing specialization.
Lookup crosses the bridge into the actual class and skips old parameter
frames thereafter. Parameter-redeclaration checks stop at that same bridge.
The original class-template spelling cannot leak into a renamed definition.

Copied member declarations link to their retained source declarations by
ordered syntax IDs, including overloads. A potentially evaluated selected use
queues the canonical instance member once. At the unit tail its definition
uses the concrete owner plus the definition's own argument-name frame, and
copies only demanded parameters/bodies into fresh dense syntax IDs. Complete
body facts are memoized once. A source with no definition remains a weak
ordinary declaration; that unit-tail absence is an explicit unavailable state,
not a false success body or an EXIT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED rejection. An unused bad
body remains uninstantiated, including a named nested class's body.

Named nested members resolve their original class source once at declaration
through indexed owner/name relationships. Their definitions, in-class or
out-of-class, remain separate absent/in-progress/complete facts. No enclosing
class replay, global retry, signature text reconstruction or namespace-string
key is introduced. Ordinary class, constructor, destructor and virtual owners
still compute concrete facts. Synthesized out-of-class special members remain
a separate pending group, rather than being guessed from an absent body.

The new `member_patterns.rs` owns qualified declaration environments and source
member correspondence; it is not a mechanical split. Sparse relationships are
keyed by compact IDs and owned by the unit graph; every instantiated syntax
node still reads its own dense facts directly. Temporary source traversal
preserves member order. Backend/optimizer/register-allocation code and
per-function lifetime boundaries are unchanged. No unsafe/FFI/compiler
fallback, environment read, test recognition or new optimization was added.
No protected file, fixture, reference, oracle source or Ralph state was edited.
The source map records the translated responsibilities without claiming any
pending compiler owner complete.

A new ownership unit test verifies two distinct argument specializations,
four member links, only two demanded body copies, deduplicated repeated use,
fresh body IDs and retained non-inline declaration facts. The focused probes
also exercise renamed heads, overloads, constructors/destructors, conversion
operators, trailing returns, namespace definitions, definition-point lookup,
unused/required nested classes, late definitions, hidden original parameter
names, parameter redeclaration and ambiguous owner diagnostics in all three
public views.

### Completed behavior and ownership review

Class shell lookup remains independent of definitions. A demanded definition
reads its declaration-owned head and successful namespace lookup ordinal,
creates a parent-linked frame containing only that head's argument aliases,
and copies the retained syntax into the unit's slabs. Each canonical class
EntityId has an absent/in-progress/complete definition state. A reentrant
request sees the in-progress class and its members so far, without restarting
it. Missing primary definitions remain retryable; failed required definitions
restore the caller and do not publish a successful memo.

The ordinary class-body owner computes member, copy/destructor and virtual
facts for the concrete class. Dependent bases wait for that definition, while
fixed bases still demand completeness at pattern definition time. Named member
classes retain separate lazy definitions. Local classes inside instantiated
function bodies retain ordinary definition-time behavior: instance membership
follows enclosing class ownership, stopping at a function/block boundary.

Member function bodies retain their parsed BodyIds while declarations and
signatures are instantiated. A potentially evaluated selected use queues that
canonical member once. The unit-tail driver copies only demanded bodies and
parameters, records facts on fresh dense IDs, and lets the unchanged lowering
owners consume them. Unused conversion functions and named nested classes
remain uninstantiated. Ordinary inline-body odr-use and output reachability
remain different facts. Vtables can add a new member-body demand: the boundary
walker suspends that function and resumes its new edges after analysis. Its
root cursor, completed function/constructor/table sets and owner-indexed
pending storage/constructor/destruction edges prevent whole-unit retries.

Class/default/member contexts restore declaration access, body, head, lookup
limit, class/template/instantiation depth, unevaluated/default-initialization
state and linkage on Result paths. Exception specification IDs retain stable
storage across nested contexts; only current pending queues are isolated.
Formation levels are immutable sparse EntityId facts and queried through a
private Result-returning interface. The logical Annex B limit is copied from
the C++ source, not inferred from host stack exhaustion.

Class ABI scopes reuse the existing canonical nominal/template nodes, including
argument substitutions and cv ownership. Presentation labels reproduce the
96-byte bounded C++ policy, first-use compact identities, scalar/cv/pointer/
reference/array/function spelling and byte sanitization. Presentation strings
never become semantic keys. Member linkage and prefer_local consume separate
unit-local/no-linkage predicates over the shared canonical type/owner DAG,
including local enum arguments; no class body is demanded by locality queries.
Source-facing type scopes omit hidden instances; semantic member names follow
class entity ownership across argument frames.

Five unit tests exercise independent definition/nested-member demand and
successful memo reuse, restoration after failed definition demand, unique
member-body copies and unused bodies, vtable-only body dependencies without
reprocessing completed boundaries, and the logical instantiation limit without
native recursion. No fixture, reference, oracle, checker policy or Ralph state
was changed. No text replay, unsafe/FFI/compiler fallback, new optimization or
backend lifetime change was introduced. Temporary IR and emission still use
the existing per-function owners.

### Remaining dependency boundary

The next shared owner is qualified current-instantiation/out-of-class owner
resolution and member pattern linking (`class_templates.cpp::outside_frames`,
`link_member_patterns`, and the qualified class/member declaration owners).
It accounts for 75 remaining first divergences at dependent qualified lookup.
Those definitions need declaration-owned parameter-name frames and links from
instance members to their retained in-class/out-of-class patterns, rather than
namespace spelling reconstruction or eager whole-template replay.

Separate remaining groups include static member definition/storage demand and
constant-reference emission, definition-bound nondependent lookup across
originally dependent bases, reentrant function signature substitution through
in-progress classes, explicit class instantiation, destructor template-ids,
member qualifiers, deeper alias/member templates and packs, and lazy default/
initializer facts not covered by the ordinary instance-body path. The 14
artifact/stream mismatches include static declarations and reentry/provenance
facts; they are not accepted as equivalent code. Exact raw diagnostics,
outputs and case IDs are in the evidence above. Every remaining failure stays
required; no handout boundary shrinks the fixed manifest.

This handoff completes the coherent class-definition/inline-member group;
it does not complete PA14, advance a stage, certify PA34 object/tool equality
or activate PA35. The optimizer and register allocator are unchanged.

## Independent review of three accepted partial checkpoints — 2026-10-16

Review entry: clean `f707a2c8`; reviewed implementation range
`b3d08217..f707a2c8`, covering `f614f945`, `2f05429d` and `f707a2c8`.
The preceding accepted implementation turn was progress (144 to 162 fixed
passes). This is a checkpoint audit of completed groups, not a requirement to
finish PA14. The stage base remains `2cc3a56f91c91d58c0c1738bd7d2ef6e60842ceb`.

Read the repository instructions, clone contract, architecture specification,
clone plan, complete handout and ledger. Independently traced the frozen C++
array formation/substitution, member binding/substitution/access, class shell
and default-argument owners, structural name keys, placeholder ABI reader and
encoder against the Rust source map and the complete implementation diff.

Reviewed implementation commit (including audit fixes): `8c257210fe773e5127f490701577c9151f8538de`.

### Findings and fixes

1. **Direct dependent value bounds were rejected at definition time.**
   `constant_named_value` treated the hidden dependent-member alias as an
   ordinary type name before recognizing `T::count` as value-dependent.
   It now returns the frozen `a value-dependent constant` NonConstant result;
   array formation retains its operand and concrete substitution performs the
   actual lookup, access check, constant evaluation and positivity check.
   This also fixes dependent value bounds in class-template argument defaults.
2. **Qualified operand ABI dispatch lost semantic resolution.** The reader
   assumed every qualified name ended in a dependent qualifier TypeId. A
   namespace/class single function, overload set, variable or enumerator could
   incorrectly select the concrete-type ABI fallback. Name keying also chose
   the first function without checking the named set's cardinality. The reader
   now consumes the recorded resolution and retains the frozen entity-reference,
   unresolved qualifier, literal and named-cast productions. An empty global
   qualifier emits `gs`, without `srE`. ABI entity naming demands no storage or
   body. Enumerator values remain distinct from enumeration type entities.
3. **Array formation omitted the unknown inner-bound rule after substitution.**
   `T[3]` and `T[sizeof(T*)]` could form an array of `int[]`, making an invalid
   candidate viable. Signature formation now applies the frozen `checked_array`
   rule after lexical element/bound substitution; concrete known inner bounds
   remain valid. `unknown-inner-before.jsonl` retains the original byte mismatch.
4. **Successful bound facts were evaluated repeatedly.** Bound substitution
   bypassed the unit's positive operand memo and could copy/analyze the same
   expression again during signature trials. Type and Bound queries now share
   explicit InProgress/Complete state under a complete key including operand
   EntityId, query kind, depth, canonical arguments and template access context.
   Results remain distinct TypeId/u64 variants; failed trials remove their entry.
   The new invariant test proves repeated bound success does not grow syntax,
   a type query has its own fact, and no body/emission is demanded.

New required regressions are in PA14's executed control lane:
`100-template-member-value-array-bound.t`,
`100-template-member-value-array-failures.t`,
`100-template-qualified-operand-abi.t`, and
`100-template-substituted-array-elements.t`.
They cover renamed bounds, exact unsigned wrapping, private/missing/non-type/
zero/negative members, retry after class completion, declaration-only demand,
class-default identity, qualified function/overload/global/variable/enumerator
ABI cases, nested class arguments/array operands, and invalid versus valid
substituted array elements. Existing fixtures were not formatted or changed.

### Responsibility and ownership review

| Frozen owner | Reviewed Rust responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `analyzer_types.cpp::checked_array/apply_suffix`, `function_templates.cpp::substitute/deduce_type`, `class_templates.cpp::substitute_bound` | `declarators.rs`, `types.rs`, `function_templates.rs`, `template_operands.rs`: separate unknown/concrete/dependent bounds; canonical/cv identity; non-deduced operands; element-first substitution and target integral validity |
| `class_templates.cpp::dependent_member_binding`, `function_templates.cpp::substitute_member`, `analyzer_access.cpp::access_point` | `dependent_members.rs`, `lookup.rs`, `friendships.rs`, `declaration_access.rs`: hidden qualifier/name identity, concrete named-scope/base lookup, non-deduced qualifiers, template-owned access and explicit-declaration exemption |
| `class_templates.cpp::class_specialization/template_default/own_arguments`, `template_records.h` | `class_templates.rs`, `templates.rs`, `graph.rs`, `syntax/cloning.rs`: canonical hidden shells, complete argument/default prefix keys, declaring scope/ordinal, fresh fact-bearing concrete defaults and independent class-definition demand |
| `template_placeholders.cpp::resolution_words`, `constant_evaluator.cpp` | `name_keys.rs`, `template_operands.rs`, `constants.rs`, `queries.rs`: typed resolution cardinality, value dependence, query-specific positive memos and restored definition context |
| `lower/placeholder_mangling.cpp`, `lower/symbol_lowering.cpp`, `abi/itanium/abi_encoder.cpp` | `lower/placeholder_mangling.rs`, `lower/symbols.rs`, `sema/lowering.rs`, `abi/encoder.rs`: recorded ABI identity and source qualifier order, exact global prefixes, deterministic emission and frozen unsupported-expression fallback |

Class shells do not acquire scopes, definitions, layouts or emitted members.
Default scope/ordinal and parameter aliases do not acquire caller locals or
access privileges. Canonical identities and lookup facts remain unit-owned;
demanded fact-bearing copies live in the existing syntax slabs and are released
with that unit. Substitution environments are compact argument overlays with
parent-linked lookup frames. Query worklists/maps have query lifetime; no
visible-environment copy or unrelated declaration scan was added. Context and
failed cache entries restore on Result paths; earlier invariant tests cover
failure restoration, hidden/canonical identities and absence of speculative
completion. The explicit access-exempt signature-cache reuse was probed against
C++ rather than changed into a different policy.

The parse-once pipeline, typed lowering and function-owned LowIR/backend
lifetimes remain intact. Primitive type/ID copies and local argument worklists
are not duplicate trees or token parsers. Diagnostic strings remain static or
lazily rendered. No new dependencies, unsafe paths, compiler/oracle delegation,
fixture recognition, environment reads, lint suppressions, mechanical source
splits or protected-policy changes were found. Production tool spellings and
Rust naming conventions are preserved. The source map records the corrections.
Optimizer, register allocator and native encoding algorithms were not changed.

### Completed group and architecture review

A specialization has one unit-owned class EntityId/TypeId and hidden binding
for its complete primary/argument key. Lookup of an omitted argument prefix
reuses the same full shell; renamed declarations share canonical argument
identity. The original name index keeps the primary. Shell publication does
not define a class scope, compute layout, instantiate member bodies or emit
anything. Shell construction performs no recursive work after allocation;
class definition states belong to the next owner and are not inferred from
the shell's presence. Dependence includes arguments and enclosing templated
class owners. A definition's own arguments can denote its current instantiation
without conflating that definition with an external dependent specialization.

Each required concrete type default gets fresh fact-bearing syntax in the
original syntax slabs and an immutable parent-linked frame binding only the
supplied parameters. Its declaring scope and lookup ordinal control names;
caller locals/access cannot supply them. The default's own source declarator
diagnoses invalid pointers/references/member types. Body, access, lookup limit,
head/depth, unevaluated/default, class and initializer contexts restore on Result
paths. Successful individual defaults and omitted prefixes have separate typed
memos; failed demands publish no successful fact. Dependent defaults use their
retained semantic pattern and signature substitution. No text or token replay
is involved. The unit tests check canonical shell reuse/hidden ownership,
absence of completion/layout/emission, and context restoration on failure.

Signature substitution preserves cv and canonical shell identity, substitutes
arguments in order, and visits only the represented argument DAG. Deduction
compares arguments of the same primary. Existing non-deduced member/operand
contexts remain separate. Lowering consumes recorded arguments for nominal ABI
names and local-type linkage. It follows dependent class arguments for the
frozen unmangled-placeholder fallback. Optimizer, register allocator and native
encoding algorithms are unchanged. No unsafe, FFI, dependency, subprocess
implementation, environment read, reference answer, global scan or owning
second syntax tree was added. `class_templates.rs` owns this semantic group,
not a mechanical split; the source map records all translated responsibilities.

Three new required fixtures are in PA14's executed control lane:
`tests/controls/100-class-template-shell-identity.t`,
`tests/controls/100-class-template-default-fact-isolation.t`, and
`tests/controls/100-class-template-invalid-default.t`. Frozen fixtures,
references, fixed manifests, oracle, protected policy and Ralph state are
unchanged. The protected single-case CLI accepts only fixed-manifest cases;
`new-controls.py` invokes its unchanged `execute_case` course/adapter function
for these supplements, retaining exact raw comparisons and native control checks.

### Completed behavior and ownership review

A dependent qualifier is a TypeId rather than a synthetic ScopeId. One hidden
binding exists per unqualified qualifier/name pair, with immutable canonical
identity shared by renamed parameters. Its entity never acquires a class scope
or ordinary name-index visibility. Substitution first resolves the qualifier,
retains a still-dependent member, then searches only the concrete class and its
required bases. Missing members, non-types, scalars, ambiguity and inaccessible
members discard signature trials; failed lookups are not globally cached, so a
class completed later can make a later trial succeed. Current definition scopes
expose only members available so far. Return/parameter/default types reuse the
ordinary type/cv/reference/array/function rules and existing specialization IDs.

The deduced primary EntityId owns the access context. An operand's positive memo
key includes that context as in C++; equivalent operands shared by a friend and
an unrelated template cannot exchange access rights. Copies retain their
original declaration-owned lookup frames and ordinal, while access remaps only
the copied scope and its descendants to the deduced template. Caller access,
friend keying, body/default/unevaluated state, template head/depth and class/member
state restore on Result paths. A namespace friend's signature keys in its
namespace, so an ordinary definition redeclares that same template and supplies
its demanded body. Explicit function declarations retain the C++ access
exemption and ordinary specialization identity/memo behavior.

Dependent bit-fields retain their existing entity/width identity with
`FieldSemantics::Dependent`; there is no invented integral sign or promotion.
Only concrete fields expose integral semantics and storage to lowering. Named
explicit function definitions reach ordinary class ABI/copy/destruction
preparation through the existing root closure, including definitions with no
call site. These are explicit demands; no unrelated function/class registry is
scanned for speculative work.

ABI production reads retained semantic qualifier/name and key-dependence facts.
The flat ABI graph records unresolved qualifier roots/levels and emits the
frozen source spelling. A key naming no dependent entity, or a production the
frozen placeholder reader cannot encode, uses that specialization's concrete
whole result/parameter component, including nested arrays/functions. Per-unit
TypeId memos bound repeated placeholder and shared type-DAG queries. This is the
C++ ABI algorithm, with no source reparsing or compiler implementation fallback.
Optimizer, register allocator and native encoding algorithms are unchanged.

The new module owns dependent member identity/substitution rather than a
mechanical source split. IDs, checked store APIs and immutable facts stay on
the existing semantic unit; demanded operand/body copies stay in its original
syntax slabs. No new dependency, unsafe, FFI, environment read, text transport,
fixture recognition or reference-answer table was introduced. The source map
records every translated responsibility, including the access and ABI owners.

Four required controls were added only to PA14's executed control lane:
`tests/controls/100-template-dependent-member-types.t`,
`tests/controls/100-template-dependent-member-failures.t`,
`tests/controls/100-template-dependent-member-operands.t`, and
`tests/controls/100-template-dependent-member-access-context.t`.
They cover renamed/chained/inherited/cv/array/default members, deferred
bit-fields, failed and later-successful trials, qualified operands and the ABI
fallback, friendship isolation, and explicit instantiation. Existing fixtures,
references, fixed manifests, protected policies and Ralph state are unchanged.
The unit invariant test checks canonical renamed chains, hidden bindings,
absence of member scopes and absence of speculative access-context demand.

### Completed group and architecture review

Distinct dependent bounds retain distinct interned types; equivalent renamed
bounds share their structural operand. Array qualification and bare-type
transforms preserve operand identity. Braced/string initializers cannot complete
a bound whose original operand remains dependent. The iterative type
canonicalizer walks both element and operand edges and keeps declaration equivalence separate from
actual lookup identity. Completing an omitted array bound never merges a
dependent bound with an unknown one. Concrete array constructors explicitly
retain no operand; existing array consumers inspect their concrete dimensions.

Signature substitution evaluates a bound only after its entire argument list
is concrete, retaining it for partial ordering or incomplete deduction. Element
substitution precedes bound evaluation, followed by ordinary validity checks.
Zero, negative, invalid constant expressions, void/reference/function/abstract
elements fail the trial; required default-instantiation errors retain their
hard-error provenance. Bounds do not deduce type parameters, while their element
types still do. Complete signatures and each query's type substitutions retain
the existing canonical success memos; no failed trial is cached as success.

Type and bound queries share the existing context-restoring operand evaluator
but return distinct enum variants. Each demanded occurrence owns its fresh
fact-bearing syntax copy. Immutable source/name interning stays shared, and
prototype environments remain parent linked. Body, lookup ordinal, template
head/depth, unevaluated/default context, class depth, initializing entity and
base/member context restore on Result paths. The existing rollback unit test
now exercises both query kinds. A new invariant test checks canonical/cv
identity, distinct unknown bounds, dependence and absence of speculative
substitution. No reparsing, visible-environment copy, whole-program scan,
unsafe code, new dependency, process fallback or environment read was added.

ABI lowering reads the typed retained operand; it performs no lookup or
semantic analysis. Array spelling in type/semantic views preserves the frozen
zero-bound presentation for deferred bounds. LowIR ordering, optimizer,
register allocator and per-function backend ownership remain unchanged.
Arithmetic and positivity checks use the existing explicit target evaluator;
new operand edges use compact TypeIds and checked storage APIs. The common
array suffix algorithm now owns its ordinary validation and deferred-bound
formation together, within the file/function audit limits.

Two new required controls live in PA14's executed control lane:
`tests/controls/100-template-array-bound-substitution.t` combines adjusted
prototype parameters, renamed declarations, cv/multidimensional arrays, local
array initializers and array-pointer results;
`tests/controls/100-template-array-bound-failures.t` checks nonpositive/division-by-zero bounds and abstract elements without body
demand. Existing fixtures, reference files, fixed manifests and policies are
unchanged. New controls supplement the fixed manifest.

### Findings and fixes

All findings below are fixed in the reviewed implementation commit; unchanged
fixture counts were not used to waive them.

1. **Unrelated prototype parameters were substituted by a shared operand.**
   A first declaration's `decltype(x+1)` could retain another parameter with
   type `decltype(required(T()))`. Substituting the shared result for a different
   function wrongly instantiated `required`'s invalid default. Operands now retain
   the frozen owner's conservative named-parameter mask, including positions in
   nested operand types. Unnamed dependent parameters below position 63 stay
   untyped in the reconstructed prototype. Unknown keys and higher positions
   remain conservative; failed attempts still publish no completed cache entry.
2. **Earlier-parameter prototype ownership was incomplete.** Parameters named
   by later parameter types lacked the positional identities already supplied to
   trailing returns. Their bindings were indexed but absent from the scope's
   enumerable binding list, so reconstructed frames lost them. Later parameters
   were also published into a scope already retained by an earlier operand.
   Both prototype paths now record ordinals; earlier scopes retain enumerable
   bindings and introduce a child frame after a newly retained operand. The
   resulting parent-linked prefixes exclude each parameter from its own type
   and exclude later parameters, without copying visible environments.
3. **Signature substitution ignored lexical result placement.** Rust substituted
   every result before parameters. For a trailing `decltype(required(x))`, this
   wrongly triggered a hard default-instantiation error even when an earlier
   invalid parameter should have discarded the candidate. The first template
   declaration now records result placement. Completed signatures substitute in
   lexical order; explicit arguments also check a leading result before parameter
   deduction. Function-type validity follows operand substitution as in C++.
   Required defaults retain their structured hard-error classification and exact
   diagnostic location/message.
4. **Suppressed specialization dumps borrowed a later prototype.** A specialization
   formed from `int f(T)` and suppressed by `extern template` printed a later
   definition's parameter name. Specializations now snapshot the parsed parameter
   clause at formation; the printer reads that fact. A demanded body still installs
   its separate copied definition parameters. This fixes bytes without lookup or
   analysis during printing.
5. **Function-type operand search could unfold a shared DAG exponentially.**
   `names_operand` now visits each `TypeId` once per query. Its new debug/release
   test checks both absent and present operands through a shared 48-level function
   type DAG, whose unfolded tree has `3^48` leaves.
6. **Dependent ABI operands lost the function-parameter clause level.** The nested
   signature regression then exposed `fp_` where C++ emits `fL0p_` for a reference
   inside a later parameter's type. Structural parameter keys now carry ordinal
   and scope level separately. The ABI graph/encoder consumes that fact directly;
   ordinary `function-param` fact input retains its existing level-zero syntax.
   The complete nested function's LowIR now matches, including its object name.

Three required regressions were added to PA14's executed control lane:
`100-template-decltype-unrelated-prototype.t` covers unrelated hard/soft operands,
nested renamed parameter types, reference results and trailing-result order;
`100-function-instantiation-declaration-parameters.t` covers formation-time
prototype names across later declarations; and
`100-template-leading-result-required-default-bad.t` checks explicit leading-result
hard-error order. All nine LowIR/semantic/type comparisons match. Both positive
controls execute successfully; the negative control rejects in the course lane.
Its type-only view, like C++, performs name checking and succeeds. All 13 controls
introduced by the reviewed checkpoints and this audit pass their individual
protected course checks (`accepted-controls-native.json`). The new nested-operand
ownership test also verifies two shared patterns and two completed substitutions.

### Algorithm, failure-path and lifetime review

Explicit instantiation keys are canonical function `EntityId`s. Private state
distinguishes extern declarations from definitions; source-ordered definition
obligations retain their diagnostic locations. Duplicate definitions do not
replace accepted state. Access deferral ends before body analysis. Named body
obligations precede implicit first-use demands; body analysis and emission remain
separate, and explicit roots enter the ordinary deduplicated lowering queue.

Default facts are keyed by canonical specialization and parameter ordinal, with
InProgress/Complete state independent of signature, body and emission. Only a
selected call's omitted parameters are instantiated, in order. Deleted/access
checks and written-argument conversions precede defaults. Defaults use their
introducing head/frame and first-head lookup ordinal; supplying arguments,
signature-only uses and unused explicit roots do not demand them. Nested calls
and runtime temporary cleanup use ordinary owners. Caller body, lookup limit,
template/class depth, unevaluated/default scope, head and initialization/base
context are restored on Result paths. A required default's diagnostic cannot
be swallowed as immediate substitution failure.

Operand keys preserve home scope, canonical types, resolved/dependent callees,
parameter positions/levels and a separate declaration-equivalence representative.
Lookup identity is not replaced by declaration identity. Successful substitutions
are cached by operand entity, depth and concrete canonical arguments; recursive
demand sees InProgress, and failed trials remove that state. The new masks and
scope prefixes prevent unrelated work rather than treating its error as success.
No output order depends on HashMap iteration.

The analyzer exclusively borrows the existing syntax slabs. Default/signature
copies have fresh fact-bearing occurrence IDs; immutable source and interning
stay shared. Query-local argument snapshots contain only IDs, with eight inline
slots and an O(argument-count) temporary vector for larger lists. Constructor
initializer maps retain Copy descriptors across slab growth. Fatal body/copy
errors drop the unit and its incomplete facts; recoverable signature/default
failures restore context before propagation. Lowering reads recorded selected
calls, conversions, default expressions and body IDs, then constructs typed LowIR.
The optimizer/backend retain no frontend ownership beyond that boundary, and
per-function backend lifetimes, optimizer and register allocator are preserved.

Reviewed the broad mutable-borrow adjustments as well as the new algorithms.
No fixture recognition, C++ implementation delegation, duplicate parser,
whole-program retry/scan, cloned visible environment, unsafe code, mechanical
file split, new dependency, environment read or lint suppression was introduced.
New state belongs to the existing unit-owned stores and helpers remain in their
owning phase. Source responsibilities are updated in `source-map.tsv`.
Numeric evaluation is unchanged; new masks/shifts and ABI level/index subtraction
are guarded, host conversions remain checked, and existing target wrap/cv/reference
probes and earlier stages pass. Diagnostic bytes, emission order and complete LowIR
bytes were compared, not merely acceptance or canonicalized course output.

### Completed behavior and ownership review

Represented free-function dependent `decltype` operands now remain untyped
patterns at definition time. Concrete signature demand copies an operand once
per canonical `(operand EntityId, depth, argument TypeIds)` key into the same
unit's flat syntax slabs and records ordinary facts on the copy. InProgress
and Complete states separate recursive demand from successful reuse. Failed
trials remove their state; no failed/incomplete lookup is cached. Supplying an
argument or naming a specialization does not demand its unrelated body,
layout or emission. Ordinary dependent member expressions on concrete PA11–13
objects reuse the existing member, access, conversion and lifetime rules.

The structural key retains namespace/class scope identity, canonical types,
parameter positions and call-name resolution. Equivalent renamed declarations
compare through a separate memoized declaration identity: a later dependent
callee lookup retains its own actual placeholder and ordinal, while declaration
comparison uses its first equivalent representative. Normal canonical type
identity stays distinct. Collision chains compare complete typed keys; map
iteration never sets semantic or output order. Compound canonical/declaration
types share one iterative DAG walk and separate dense memos. These changes
preserve O(1) equality after first computation, without global invalidation.

Substitution reconstructs only the introducing head's bindings and its prototype
parameters in parent-linked frames. It restores body, lookup limit, template
head/depth, class depth, unevaluated/default context, initializing entity and
base/member-initializer context on Result paths. The pattern's parsed syntax,
source and interned names stay shared; its fact-bearing copies have fresh IDs.
There is no grammar replay, whole-unit syntax clone or per-node pattern lookup.

Call partial ordering now substitutes only the parameter types corresponding
to written arguments, matching C++; it does not analyze a return expression
with synthetic ordering types. Function-type ordering skips placeholder results
as the frozen owner does. `decltype` operands are non-deduced contexts. Defaults
of template type parameters substitute against the earlier argument prefix,
so a result default such as `U = decltype(T())` does not wait on U itself.

A signature's immediate-expression failure can discard a candidate. A default
argument that this expression's selected call requires is a separate required
instantiation: its error propagates. The new structured
`InstantiationDiagnostic` preserves that distinction without string tests,
rendering, allocation or changing the eventual diagnostic. The existing default
failure-restoration unit test now checks this stronger error classification.

The ABI reader consumes the structural key directly. It supports the represented
integral/parameter/name operands, explicit type arguments, unary/binary/conditional
operators, subscript/member/call expressions, reference-aware casts, braced and
conversion lists, prefix/postfix increment/decrement and sizeof/alignof/noexcept
forms. Fixed type-query ABI literals consume existing target layout facts.
These are inherited ABI serialization rules, not new optimizer transformations.
The optimizer, register allocator, emission ordering and per-function backend
lifetimes are unchanged. No dependency, unsafe code, FFI, subprocess compiler
implementation, environment read, test recognition or lint suppression was added.
The three new implementation files own operand demand, expression dependence
and dependent ABI serialization respectively; the final source audit has zero
findings across 278 files.

SHA-256 `c4c44d806ca2a7ba77b579b6d90d6a14134cd192278c2147c40413cc9db74033`.

Read the repository guide, clone contract, specification, Rust clone plan,
PA14 handout and current ledger; consulted these frozen C++ owners directly:

| C++ owner | Rust responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `analyzer_initialization.cpp::record_template_default_arguments/type_default_arguments`, `class_templates.cpp::template_head` | `sema/default_arguments.rs`, `sema/templates.rs`: keep defaults as untyped patterns, with the introducing parameter scope/head and the first template head's lookup ordinal |
| `class_templates.cpp::instantiate_default_argument/instantiation_frame/InstantiationContext` | `sema/template_defaults.rs`, `sema/template_bodies.rs`: selected-parameter demand, fresh expression/fact IDs, declaration-owned argument frames, ordinary initialization and restoration of caller context on Result paths |
| `analyzer_calls.cpp::call/default_argument_visible`, `function_templates.cpp::specialize` | `sema/procedural_calls.rs`, `sema/function_templates.rs`: reject deleted/inaccessible selections before defaults, convert written operands before omitted defaults, filter late-added defaults by definition context, then request bodies/emission |
| `analyzer.cpp`, `syntax/ast_clone.cpp::SyntaxCloner` and semantic syntax readers | `sema/analyzer.rs`, `sema/call_arguments.rs`, `syntax/cloning.rs`: exclusive access to the same unit-owned syntax, indexed Copy descriptor reads and short query-owned argument snapshots across synchronous demand |

### Completed group and ownership review

For represented free-function type-parameter signatures and default syntax,
only a selected call's omitted parameters instantiate defaults, in parameter
order. Supplying all arguments, selecting another overload, naming a signature
or explicitly instantiating an unused function does not instantiate its unused
defaults. Extern suppression of a function's body does not suppress a default
that a call needs. Calls inside defaults may demand other defaults and bodies;
unevaluated calls still instantiate defaults without demanding the selected
function's body. Default evaluation runs anew at each runtime call even though
its semantic facts are computed once per canonical specialization/parameter.

A private unit-owned store keys InProgress/Complete state by `EntityId` and
parameter ordinal; failed entries are removed. Successful copies are published
on the specialization's ordinary default vector and consumed directly by
lowering and semantic views. The primary's expression stays untyped. Each copy
has fresh `ExprId`, name and type-syntax IDs in the existing flat slabs; source,
spellings and interned identities remain shared. No parser replay, pattern
redirection on expression reads or second semantic tree was introduced.

The default's introducing head/frame remains separate from a later qualified
or renamed function definition. The first head's ordinal is retained even when
a later redeclaration introduces a default. Caller body, lookup limit, default
scope, unevaluated/template/class depth, active head, initializing entity and
base-object context are restored after analysis, including failure paths.
Ordinary braced, scalar, pointer, function-pointer, reference, class conversion
and class-copy rules supply the actual parameter initialization facts.

Analyzer syntax access is now an exclusive borrow so a call can append its
copy synchronously. Readers that cross such calls retain compact IDs or local
Copy descriptors instead of slab references. Query argument snapshots keep up
to eight IDs inline; larger lists own a temporary contiguous vector. They copy
only the query's IDs, cost O(argument count), and die with the query. Constructor
initializer indexes similarly retain Copy descriptors rather than pointers
into an appendable slab. Existing algorithms, source/emission order, per-function
backend lifetimes, optimizer and register allocator remain intact. There is no
whole-program scan/retry, environment read, dependency, unsafe code, subprocess
implementation, fallback answer or lint suppression. One existing placeholder
ownership test moved into its owning `placeholders.rs` module after the borrow
adjustments pushed the general test file over its audited size cap; no audit
policy was relaxed and the test is still executed in debug and release.

## Explicit function-instantiation checkpoint — 2026-10-16

Implementation entry HEAD: `06edaf1b052f7d174c1f6cdb01bfdc51c017cf4e`, clean.
The stage base and last-audited commit above are unchanged. The previous goal
turn produced an independent audit with new validation evidence (progress).
This checkpoint completes the explicit **free-function** instantiation group
for the currently represented type-parameter signatures and bodies. PA14
remains partial: **125/418** fixed cases pass, **293** fail, compared with
**123/418** and **295** failures at entry. Every **123/123** previous passing
identity is retained; no case or protected input was removed or changed.

Read the repository guide, clone contract, specification, Rust clone plan,
PA14 handout and ledger. Consulted the frozen C++ implementation directly:

| C++ owner | Rust responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `template_specializations.cpp::declared_specialization/instantiated_function/explicit_function_instantiation/InstantiationSuppressed` | `sema/function_templates.rs`, `sema/explicit_instantiations.rs`: full declared-type deduction with optional explicit arguments, indexed named candidates and canonical signature reuse; access-exempt declaration checks; namespace, specifier, operator and duplicate diagnostics; per-specialization extern/definition state |
| `class_templates.cpp::explicit_instantiation`, `analyzer.cpp::analyze` | `sema/analyzer.rs`, `sema/template_bodies.rs`: single-entity dispatch, named-definition validation at unit tail before implicit body demands, late definitions, deleted definitions and extern suppression |
| `analyzer_types.cpp::declarator` | `sema/declarators.rs`: typed placement mode lets the instantiation owner enforce its namespace rule after declaration matching |
| `lower/program_lowering.cpp::lower_unit/emit_definition`, `lower/symbol_lowering.cpp::function_metadata` | `sema/lowering.rs`, `lower/mod.rs`, `lower/symbols.rs`: explicit roots append after implicit emission demand drains, ordinary deduplication and per-function streaming, weak linkage independent of inline hints, object-root metadata only on definitions |

The earliest divergent owner was the explicit-instantiation declaration stub.
A declaration now resolves to a canonical specialization `BindingId`/`EntityId`
through the existing primary/argument-`TypeId` signature cache, rather than
publishing another declaration or selecting a function by its rendered name.
A private typed store holds declaration/definition state and a source-ordered
list of named definition obligations with their diagnostic locations. Repeated
extern declarations share state; a definition overrides them; a second
definition or an extern after a definition reports the C++ diagnostic without
changing the accepted state.

Named definitions are analyzed before the ordinary body-demand queue, in the
oracle's order, and may find a template body declared later in the unit. An
unavailable definition is diagnosed at the naming declaration; a deleted one
requires no body. End-of-unit extern state suppresses non-inline implicit body
instantiation even when a use appeared earlier. Inline specializations retain
ordinary body demand. Signature formation, body analysis and output emission
remain independent: unused named definitions are checked and emitted, unused
function defaults stay uninstantiated, and an unused extern specialization
emits nothing. Explicit definitions keep ordinary template weak binding and
add `object_root=yes` only to emitted definitions.

Access deferral is scoped to declaration matching and restored on Result
paths, so private argument/type names are exempt without granting access to
the instantiated body. The existing syntax copier owns fresh body/fact IDs in
the same unit slabs; immutable source and names stay shared. The new queues
visit only indexed named instances and existing demands, once each; no global
scan/retry, reparsing, second semantic tree, environment read, subprocess
implementation, dependency, unsafe code, lint suppression or optimization was
introduced. All lowering/backend ownership and optimizer/allocator behavior
are preserved.

### Finding, fix and regressions

**Fixed:** builtin class conversions were absent from semantic views. This
reproduced the two unclaimed converted-class dump differences in the operator
checkpoint and also affected parenthesized identifiers, member operands, factory
calls and unary operators. LowIR already used the correct retained conversion.
C++ replaces the occurrence's facts with the selected conversion call while
keeping its original receiver. Rust deliberately keeps those original facts
and a typed `FunctionConversion` edge, but its printer used only the original.

`0d659526` projects the recorded result/category and selected function into a
short-lived presentation value for `ConversionUse::Builtin`. Member operands
print the conversion designation; converted call operands print the selected
callee. Parentheses follow the existing canonical expression root. The graph,
AST, conversion selection and lowering remain unchanged. No name lookup,
source reparsing, synthetic syntax or diagnostic-text inference was added.
`docs/port/source-map.tsv` records the added presentation responsibility.

The new debug/release ownership regression
`builtin_conversion_view_preserves_receiver_facts_and_selected_demand` checks
five expression shapes against C++ presentation, retained original receiver
types/categories/selected bindings, and non-demand of invalid losing template
bodies. The new executed control
`pa12/tests/controls/100-builtin-conversion-expression-shapes.t` belongs to PA12,
the earliest owner of ordinary class conversions; it checks all five forms and
exactly one conversion side effect per operand. Its protected comparison matches
LowIR hash `99359fdda45ff88d155de9d6c366e4c308f24f0f1c755da3b9989b6742d2570a`
and its native execution passes. No frozen input/reference, protected policy,
C++ source, dependency, lint policy or Ralph state changed.

### Algorithm, ownership and failure-path review

Friend references select from indexed named function sets, compare the whole
canonical declared type, and publish only hidden binding references to the
canonical specialization. Repeated grants reuse one entity; primaries and
siblings receive no grant. Rejected/defaulted/inline/ambiguous declarations
follow the existing Result paths and ordinary initializer checks. They request
no function default, body or emission. Class-template friend placeholders remain
patterns awaiting the unfinished containing-class specialization owner.

Signature keys retain primary `EntityId` and canonical argument `TypeId`s;
`InProgress` prevents recursive signature work and successful signatures are
memoized. Substitution failures remove their provisional entry before returning.
Body demand is separate: each entity enters a first-use queue once, has explicit
in-progress/complete state, and may append further demands. Undefined templates
retain declarations; signature-only friends and unevaluated uses request no body.
The definition's own head/frame and binding ordinal govern name lookup, including
renamed/qualified definitions and ordinary definition-time calls.

Body copies allocate fresh fact-bearing IDs into the existing flat unit slabs;
immutable source/spelling/identifier storage stays shared. Names and type syntax
with context-specific dense facts receive fresh IDs, as do local declarations
and classes. Nested list scratch rewinds on successful list completion; any fatal
copy/analysis error drops the unit, so partially appended nodes or an in-progress
body cannot escape as a successful graph. Temporary analysis sessions borrow the
slabs, move the same Graph, restore body/noexcept context on Result paths, and
release session scratch. Unit-owned ordinals, block linkage obligations and
complete-class conversion caches survive sessions because their keys do.

Storage/call/constructor/destruction boundaries are collected on the unit and
visited after the body queue drains. They follow reached owner edges rather
than retrying every declaration per body. Operator candidates reuse ordinary,
member and ADL indexes; ties and duplicate builtin exclusion inspect canonical
primary identity. Postfix deduction never manufactures a zero as a deduction
source. Losing candidates do not demand bodies. Lowering consumes recorded
calls/conversions/body IDs and ABI arguments; weak emission remains independent
of inline preference. Source order, first-use demand order and final emission
order remain explicit and deterministic.

No fixture-specific behavior, oracle delegation, duplicate parser, global
registry scan/retry, copied visible environment, unsafe path, blanket lint
suppression, mechanical split or backend lifetime change was found in the
reviewed increments. The new printer projection is a local Copy value and an
existing typed-edge lookup, not a second semantic tree. Numeric algorithms were
unchanged; unsigned wrap, cv-sensitive parameters, reference results, diagnostic
bytes and declaration/emission order were independently compared. Existing
optimizer and register allocator behavior is preserved.

ranking. The implicit zero remains an existing lowering responsibility.

ties with intentionally invalid unused bodies, and postfix increment/decrement.

## Demanded function-template body checkpoint — 2026-10-16

PA14 remains partial at **116/418**, with **302** fixed-manifest failures,
down from **353** at entry. All **65/65** previous passing identities remain;
the 418-case manifest and its coverage are unchanged. This checkpoint implements
body materialization and ordinary checking for function-template specializations
whose signatures and semantic operations are represented by the existing owners. It does not advance PA14.

Read the repository guide, clone contract, specification, assignment handout,
Rust clone plan and this ledger. Consulted the frozen C++ owners directly:

| C++ owner | Rust owner and translated responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `sema/analyzer.cpp::analyze`, `class_templates.cpp::instantiate_definition/instantiation_frame/definition_context` | `sema/template_bodies.rs`, `sema/analyzer.rs`: deduplicated first-use unit-tail queue, separate body states, definition-owned parameter heads, alias frames, namespace declaration limits and ordinary semantic sessions |
| `syntax/ast_clone.cpp::SyntaxCloner` | `syntax/cloning.rs`: fresh fact-bearing expressions, declarations, declarators, names, types, statements and local classes in the same flat unit slabs; copied parameter clauses, initializers, handlers and ambiguous readings; shared source bytes and identifier identities |
| `sema/analyzer_bodies.cpp::function_body` | `sema/analyzer.rs`: specialized parameter object types preserve declaration cv and array/function adjustment; bodies reuse ordinary scopes, conversions, class metadata and access checks |
| `sema/function_templates.cpp::specialize`, `class_templates.cpp` exception substitution | `sema/function_templates.rs`, `sema/template_bodies.rs`: canonical argument retention, primary inline/exception metadata, copied dependent exception operands evaluated separately on body demand, source parameter object cv preserved independently of the canonical call signature; signature-demanded dependent exceptions remain status 86 |
| `lower/symbol_lowering.cpp::function_encoding/function_metadata` | `lower/symbols.rs`, `sema/lowering.rs`: template-id ABI arguments and primary signature/result encoding, weak emission independent of the primary's inline hint, existing implicit-member metadata retained |
| `lower/function_lowering.cpp`, `sema/semantics_dump.cpp` | `lower/function.rs`, `sema/semantics_dump.rs`: read the recorded specialization body; emit/print it through existing demanded-function paths |
| `sema/layout.cpp` and analyzer-owned linkage/conversion state | `sema/graph.rs`, `sema/layout.rs`: pending boundary work, block-linkage obligations, conversion-set caches and local type ordinals live across sessions on the same unit graph |

The existing `(primary EntityId, canonical argument TypeIds)` memo still forms
one signature. A potentially evaluated selected use queues that specialization's
body independently of emission. Signature-only friends and unevaluated uses do
not queue it. The queue observes a definition declared later in the unit and can
append further demands without rescanning unrelated declarations. Repeated and
recursive calls reuse the entity and body state. A template with no definition
retains a declaration; no successful empty-body substitute is synthesized.

A definition records its own template head, even after earlier heads were merged
or a qualified declarator introduced a lookup bridge. Instantiation binds only
that head's aliases into a frame parented at the definition's context. The stored
namespace ordinal excludes later overloads and directives. The specialization
entity supplies friendship/access identity, so a friend grant to a sibling cannot
authorize its private accesses.

The driver exclusively lends the syntax slabs for copying between immutable
analysis sessions. Every read of an instantiated occurrence uses a normal dense
ID and its own facts, with no pattern lookup or environment map per node read.
The graph owns all persistent facts, while session scratch is reclaimed per body.
Pending function/storage/constructor/destruction boundaries are visited once after
the body worklist finishes, avoiding a full demand traversal per specialization.
Lowering retains its existing per-function lifetimes, emission order, optimizer
and register allocator. No source reparsing, second semantic tree, unsafe code,
FFI, subprocess implementation, new dependency or environment access was added.

Three ownership tests cover body reuse, distinct copied IDs/scopes, definition-time
lookup, qualified renamed heads, hidden argument frames, unused inline demand,
unevaluated/signature-only non-demand, retained undefined declarations and drained
boundary work. Existing PA14 executed fixtures cover all 51 gained identities. The new executed
PA14 control `tests/controls/100-dependent-noexcept-const-parameter-bad.t` locks
the cv-sensitive exception-prototype regression. No frozen input, reference,
checker policy or Ralph state changed.
