### C++ owners, translated behavior and typed data flow

Read `AGENTS.md`, `CLONE_CONTRACT.md`, `spec.md`, `pa14/README.md`,
`docs/rust-clone-plan.md` and this ledger. Consulted the frozen implementation
under `/home/vishvananda/work/references/v5rust-cpp/dev/src/sema/` directly:

| Frozen C++ owner | Rust owner and responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `analyzer_declarations.cpp::declare_function/qualified_friend/friend_specialization` | `sema/function_names.rs`, `sema/friendships.rs`: friend template-id routing, default/inline prohibition before selection, canonical specialization grant, and hidden unprinted declaration references; qualified/template-id friends of class-template definitions retain patterns without selecting a specialization |
| `template_specializations.cpp::declared_specialization/deduce_for_declaration/declaration_pattern` | `sema/function_templates.rs`: explicit type arguments, deduction from the complete declared function type, forwarding-reference adjustment, explicit-substitution retry, default type-argument completion, exact canonical type agreement and candidate selection |
| `function_templates.cpp::at_least_as_specialized` | `sema/function_templates.rs`: reuse the represented-signature partial-ordering owner in both directions; declaration ambiguity follows that test without the separate call-reference tie-breaker |
| `analyzer_declarations.cpp::simple_declarator/default_definition`, `analyzer_virtual.cpp::pure_specifier` | `sema/function_names.rs`: retain ordinary initializer checks on specialization-friend declarations; an invalid initializer cannot be accepted by the new declaration path |
| `lookup.cpp::declare`, hidden `Binding` publication rules | `sema/graph.rs`: private hidden-function insertion retains origin/scope/access metadata while publishing no ordinary, qualified, ADL or printed-scope entry |

The explicit argument syntax is resolved once into canonical `TypeId`s. Only
functions in the indexed named lookup result participate; ordinary functions
are excluded from template selection. Each candidate starts with its explicit
argument prefix and deduces from the full declared type, including the result.
A failed deduction with explicit arguments retries after substitution, preserving
reference collapsing. The completed specialization must have the exact canonical
declared type. Multiple candidates follow the C++ mutual partial-ordering test,
including its ambiguity for competing mutable/const-reference patterns.

Signature construction reuses the existing unit-owned `(primary EntityId,
canonical argument TypeIds)` table and `InProgress`/`Complete` states. Repeated
friends share one specialization entity while retaining separate hidden binding
references. Friendship edges use the selected `EntityId`; neither the primary
nor other specializations gain that access. Signature formation does not request
a function default argument, exception specification, body, layout or emission.
The original template syntax and name environment remain retained for those
independent demands. Type template defaults needed to complete a signature
continue through their existing owner.

A qualified friend or friend template-id in a class-template definition remains
a hidden declaration pattern. It can name a function not yet declared; selection
and access grants wait for the containing specialization. The retained class
syntax owns that later replay. No copy of the tree, token replay, global retry,
unrelated declaration scan, new dependency, environment read, unsafe code,
FFI or compiler subprocess was introduced. Existing lowering, optimization,
register allocation, emission ordering and per-function backend lifetimes were
not changed. `docs/port/source-map.tsv` records the translated responsibilities.

Three ownership regressions cover canonical identity reuse, separate grants for
`int`/`char` specializations, absence of primary/sibling grants, no namespace or
ADL publication, retained untyped function defaults, no body/emission demand,
unchanged syntax slab lengths, failure-path state restoration and unresolved
nested/qualified class-template friend patterns. They run in both debug and
release workspace suites. Existing executed PA14 fixtures already cover the two
new exact rejections; no frozen fixture, reference, policy or Ralph state changed.

## Typed data flow, demand and architecture review

A namespace function-template declaration is indexed by its `ScopeId`, typed
callable key, complete canonical `TypeId` (including the result), and optional
interned template-head `ShapeId`. Ordinary functions retain their result-erased
signature. Different return types, template parameter counts, and ordinary versus
template declarations no longer collapse into one entity. Equivalent redeclarations
update the existing entity's indexed binding in its original overload position.
The index excludes templates imported from another namespace while the ordinary
visible overload edges retain those imports. Class-local searches visit only the
named overload family and apply the same full-type/head-shape identity; a matching
import is hidden by its parameter signature and head shape, in the C++ order.
Qualified namespace definitions use direct signature probes, and both namespace
and class definitions require a matching declaration.

Redeclaration agreement has one callable owner and preserves return, deleted,
redefinition, linkage and constexpr diagnostics before default/head merging. A later
function-template declaration cannot add an ordinary function default, including
when its template head also illegally repeats a type default. First-declaration
function defaults retain original `ExprId` patterns on the canonical function;
a later parameter clause omitting a default cannot erase it. The original first
head retains the parameter-name environment. Defaults stay unconverted and
unevaluated until a specialization uses one; that demand still returns explicit
missing implementation. No unsupported successful body or default is synthesized.

Definition-time default-name work retains original clause/type/scope identities
and template depth. Ordinary members of a class template use the existing
source-ordered complete-class queue, with saved depth restored on Result paths.
As frozen `finish_members` does, default-only entries of member/friend function
templates stay deferred, including an undeclared name in such an unused default.
A lookup already stopped at a typed dependent qualifier does not select a member;
it still validates retained template-argument syntax. Other lookup diagnostics and
missing-specialization boundaries propagate unchanged.

The ownership regression in `sema/function_names.rs` proves distinct canonical
entities for ordinary and return/head-shape template overloads, index reuse for
renamed equivalent declarations, persistent defaults despite the latest clause
omitting them, absent default expression facts, absent root/body demand and
unchanged syntax expression/declaration slab sizes. Namespace identity probes
remain indexed; no unrelated declaration scan, global retry or invalidation was
introduced. All new state is semantic-unit or declaration-completion owned.
No new dependency, unsafe, FFI, compiler subprocess, environment read, fixture
recognition, reference answer or textual phase transport was introduced. Optimizer,
register allocator, lowering/emission order and per-function backend lifetimes
are unchanged.

## Earlier dependency boundary and remaining groups

The completed group is declaration identity and default-pattern/name handling
for represented canonical signature types. It does not claim full dependent
expression equivalence or demanded template instantiation. Earliest divergent
clusters remain class/alias specialization lookup (158), template specialization
lookup (91), demanded function-template bodies (55), dependent qualified lookup
(11), special-member signatures (7), dependent base formation (7), and explicit
instantiation (5). The exact cluster inventory is in `clusters-final.json`.

Function-template bodies remain the next independent signature-to-demand
boundary. Consult frozen `class_templates.cpp::instantiate_definition`: retain
the defining parameter environment and lookup context; materialize fact-bearing
parsed syntax once per complete canonical specialization key, record InProgress
and Done body states, and reuse ordinary semantic/lowering owners. Default
arguments, exception specifications, body analysis and emission are separate
requests. No token replay or successful empty body may replace those boundaries.
Class specialization also needs independently memoized declaration, completion,
layout, member body, initializer/static storage and emission facts, with local
and nested class identities distinct per specialization.

Dependent decltype operands still need the C++ `template_placeholders.cpp`
structural placeholder and equivalent-representative machinery, preserving
parameter positions, definition-time callee bindings and declaration equivalence.
The newly passing parameter-position ambiguity uses already represented canonical
result types; it does not prove those broader expression identities. Remaining
fixed-result merge/renaming successes mostly reach demanded bodies. Preserve
the oracle's lookup/deduction/partial-ordering and exact codegen as those owners
are ported. Out-of-class/nested/static class-template definitions remain later
class-specialization work.

`analyzer_classes.cpp` and `lookup.cpp`. The review covers fixed expression
validation and statement contexts; bounded definition-time call-name/ADL and
fixed-base access checks; and represented function-template declaration
identity, redeclarations and default patterns. It does not certify demanded
specialization bodies, class-specialization completion or future assignment
behavior.

### Finding and fix

**PA14-A1: exception agreement ran after template default merging.** An earlier
`template<class T> int f(T,int) noexcept;` followed by
`template<class U> int f(U,int=1);` must report
`redeclaration with an incompatible exception-specification`. Rust instead
reported the later function-default prohibition. Repeating a template-head
default similarly hid exception incompatibility behind
`default template argument redefined`. Both tools returned a real exit 1;
the diagnostic bytes, including the applicable source position, differed.
`extra-before.json`, `extra-before.jsonl` and retained mismatch directories
record **15 mismatches in 24 compound probes**. The defect is within the
accepted redeclaration/default group even though no frozen case changed.

Frozen `Analyzer::redeclare_function` checks return/member/deleted/redefinition,
linkage and constexpr agreement, then exception compatibility, before
`declare_function` checks later function defaults or merges template heads.
Rust now follows that order. `ValueScope` borrows the original `DeclaratorInfo`
and the visibility limit captured before publication. Its lifetime ends with
the declaration query. Existing template declarations call the existing typed
exception owner from `agree_function_redeclaration`; a first template declaration
records its specification once before default-name checks. The outer simple
and definition owners skip that already recorded template work. Ordinary
function exception ownership is preserved. No speculative expression facts,
extra successful specification query or new cross-entity cache was introduced.
The source map records this responsibility against `analyzer_declarations.cpp`.

The new unit regression checks incompatible literal and dependent exception
specifications: the earlier indexed function binding, canonical head and saved
function default remain published; lookup visibility and template context are
restored; syntax slab sizes, expression facts and emission roots remain
unchanged. Four new fixtures live in PA14's executed control lane, which owns
the template redeclaration/default behavior introduced by these checkpoints:

- `tests/controls/100-template-exception-before-function-default-bad.t`
- `tests/controls/100-template-exception-before-head-default-bad.t`
- `tests/controls/100-template-dependent-exception-before-defaults-bad.t`
- `tests/controls/100-template-exception-redeclaration-keeps-default.t`

All four pass the owning checker with protected exact invocation receipts.
The course run also executes all four. Existing frozen inputs, references,
checker policy, C++ sources and Ralph state are unchanged.

### Independent algorithm and ownership review

The two new semantic modules have cohesive C++ responsibilities, rather than
mechanical file splits. Fixed validation borrows original parsed `ExprId`s and
uses optional canonical `TypeId`, category, possible-null and constant flags.
It intentionally preserves C++'s conservative possible-null flag rather than
inventing a concrete constexpr value. Numeric typing and scalar/reference
conversions reuse the prior owning algorithms. Seed traversal and expression
validation preserve operand/error order and opaque syntax boundaries; neither
publishes runtime conversions, selects demanded template bodies nor clones ASTs.
The 8/16-argument, 32-level name-probe and 200-level validation bounds are the
frozen C++ semantic probes, not calibrated native-stack limits. Explicit probes
cover their boundaries, integral/floating/character/null operands, pointer and
reference results, overload/default viability and compound diagnostic ordering.

Name-only ADL supplies canonical types to the existing association worklist;
its query scratch is cleared on Result paths. It follows indexed associated
class/base/namespace/hidden-friend edges without scanning unrelated declarations.
Fixed-base checks retain complete-class access deferral, friendship and complete
base-list context; pending access is reclaimed at the declaration boundary.
Namespace template identity remains indexed by scope, callable key, canonical
complete type and interned head shape. Imported templates retain visible lookup
edges without becoming that namespace's own declaration identities. Member
searches stay within the named overload family. Redeclarations preserve the
original overload position and first head/default environment.

Default patterns retain original syntax and declaration scope; complete-class
name checking remains on the existing source-ordered completion queue, with
member-template default-only deferral matching frozen `finish_members`.
Failure-path review covered saved depth, lookup visibility, implicit-initializer
suppression, body context, declaration access and ADL scratch restoration.
Unsupported demanded bodies/defaults and specialization lookup still return
explicit missing implementation (status 86), which is never accepted as a C++
rejection. No fake body, fixture shortcut, oracle delegation, duplicate parser,
textual phase transport, unsafe code, dependency, new lint suppression or
production environment read was introduced. Production tool spellings remain
compatible; Rust identifiers use Rust conventions. Primary templates emit no
unrequested definitions. Existing lowering order, code generation, optimizer,
register allocator and per-function backend lifetimes are preserved.

## PA17 checkpoint audit: defaulted explicit roots (2026-10-17)

The independent PA17 partial audit found shared explicit-root defects within
PA14's supported type-template surface. Frozen `analyzer.cpp` accepts a deleted
function's definition without demanding its body; `analyzer_copy.cpp` publishes
defaulted copy deletion and retains no callable for a deleted subobject
assignment selection. `program_lowering.cpp::demand_entry` independently emits
first-defaulted explicit roots. Rust now preserves those separate facts in
`sema/copy_properties.rs`, `assignment.rs`, `template_bodies.rs` and the typed
`lowering.rs` interface. `lower/assignment.rs` omits an empty subobject's
storage transfer when its recorded selection has no callable. Ordinary
non-deleted defaulted assignment roots reuse their existing synthesized plans.
No parsing, optimizer or register allocator change is involved.

Two new required controls execute in this earliest owning lane:
`tests/controls/300-explicit-defaulted-assignment-definition.t` and
`tests/controls/300-explicit-class-deleted-defaulted-member-roots.t`.
The PA17 audit's V artifact root
(`/home/vishvananda/work/private/v5rust/artifacts/pa17-checkpoint-audit-v`)
retains their focused course checks, exact adapter comparisons and final
PA14 fixed/full-course rerun in `regressions-results.json`, `pa14-results.json`
and `course-pa14/`. Its `evidence-audit.json` asserts discovery, exact matching
and final binary identity. See `docs/port/pa17.md` for the full findings,
resource costs, source review and final validation commands. PA14's historical
base and last-audited markers above remain unchanged.
